### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 澳洲央行（RBA）於 8 /11 貨幣政策會議宣佈維持現金利率目標於 4.35%不變，
為連續第二次會議按兵不動。此決議符合市場預期，主要為評估失業率上升及房
市降溫對抑制通膨的效果，行長蜜雪兒·布洛克（Michelle Bullock）與委員會維
持偏鷹派的指引，強調若通膨上行風險持續，不排除未來進一步升息的可能性。
 美國上週密集公布的關鍵數據，徹底印證了通膨與消費雙雙降溫的宏觀趨勢。在
通膨數據方面，7 月份消費者物價指數（CPI）僅微增 0.1%，核心 CPI年增率降
至 2.5%；而生產者物價指數（PPI）更意外持平，核心 PPI 回落至 4.7%，顯示
整體物價上行壓力已獲得實質性的緩解。另一方面隨著大額退稅帶來的提振效
應全面消退，7 月零售銷售大減 0.6%，不僅創下 14個月以來的最大降幅，更凸
顯出高物價對民眾購買力的嚴重侵蝕。疊加 8 月份密西根大學消費者信心指數

重挫至 51.0 的慘淡表現，美國消費引擎顯然已陷入急凍狀態。通膨放緩與消費
動能熄火的雙重打擊，導致芝商所（CME）FedWatch工具顯示，市場押注聯準
會在 9 月會議上按兵不動的機率已逼近七成。
 美伊針對荷姆茲海峽的控制權衝突持續升溫。伊朗宣稱與阿曼達成臨時通航方
案，但強調海峽重啟取決於美國條件；美國總統川普則強硬宣稱美國已透過封鎖
管控海峽，並揚言將其列為美國領土，引發德黑蘭當局強烈反擊與區域航運緊
張。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Aug/17/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 31.991，低點在 32.302，以 32.046作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 2 角 4.2 分。 
上週台幣震盪走升，周一早盤反映美國非農就業人數疲弱，美元指數僅小幅反
彈，加以台股走揚、外資匯入之下，台幣走升，週二，市場等待美國通膨就業數據
出爐，市場交投謹慎，台幣區間窄幅震盪；週間，由於美國通膨數據符合預期，淡
化市場對於九月聯準會升息預期，外資連續買超台股，帶動台幣走升，惟逢低仍有
進口商買盤抑制台幣升值速度，臨近周末，美國公布最新 PPI 數據持平，市場持續
淡化聯準會升息預期，加以外資買超台股並匯入，台幣持續走升，最終在央行調節
之下收在 32.046。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間偏升。就國際市場而言，美國最新CPI及 PPI皆未出現惡化，
顯示通膨並未失控，淡化市場對於聯準會 9 月升息預期；就台灣市場來看，上周外
資連續五日買超台股，台股持續走揚，加以投信亦匯入有利台幣表現，惟匯價逢低
吸引不少進口商買美元，央行亦提供流動性，減緩台幣升值速度，綜合以上，預估
本週台幣走勢區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.800-32.200

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Aug/17/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣高檔震盪。周初，美國非農就業數據不及預期，國際美元僅小幅反
彈，人民幣中間價 6.7884升至近三年半新高，惟人民幣已升至年內高點附近，加以
市場等待美國通膨數據出爐，市場觀望氣氛濃厚、成交清淡，日內波幅狹窄，匯價
大致維持在 6.7430-6.7500區間窄幅震盪；週間，美國陸續公布CPI及 PPI數據，皆
符合市場預期，市場淡化聯準會升息預期，人民幣最高升至 6.7397，終場境外人民
幣收在 6.7443，週線貶值 22點，境內人民幣則收在 6.7422，週線升值 40點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣高檔震盪。就國際市場來看，美國通膨數據大致符合預期，緩
解市場對於聯準會九月升息的擔憂，目前 FedWatch Tool 顯示九月升息機率僅剩三
成，美元指數於 99.500附近震盪；就中國國內市場來看，出口商實質拋匯需求仍有
望支撐人民幣表現，加以上週中間價走升，人行透過中間價釋放人民幣偏強信號，
惟人民幣已升至年內高點，上漲動能趨緩；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 小時圖
中，均線趨平，顯示人民幣上漲至年內高點附近轉趨橫盤整理。綜合以上，預估本
週人民幣高檔震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.7300-6.7550

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Aug/17/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 USD/JPY震盪偏升。週初因美伊不確定性再次升溫，油價上漲推升通膨預
期，美元自近期的低點反彈，帶領USD/JPY上升到 159上方；週間，匯市在CPI公
布前以觀望為主，USD/JPY於 159.40附近盤整，反映油價偏高對能源進口國日本仍
構成壓力；臨近週末，美國公布 CPI與 PPI 數據使市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期
大幅減少，USD/JPY一度回落至 158.58，終場收在 159.30，週線上漲 156點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、受通膨壓力、AI 強勁需求
及弱勢日圓影響，消息指出日本央行將於 9 月升息，並考慮加快後續緊縮節奏。因
企業與家庭的通膨預期已逼近 2%目標，決策層難以久候，目前市場押注日本央行9
月升息機率已逼近八成 ， 預計將支撐日幣走勢 。 二 、 美國7月零售銷售意外下滑 0.6%，
創 14 個月最大降幅且為九個月來首降；核心零售亦跌 0.4%，凸顯退稅效應消退與
高物價導致消費急凍。同時，8 月密西根大學消費者信心指數重挫至 51.0。消費支
出放緩促使機構下調 Q3 經濟預期，並使市場押注聯準會 9 月按兵不動的機率躍升
至近七成，緊縮預期大幅降溫，短線不利USD/JPY走勢。三、就技術面而言，日線
圖中，USD/JPY於美日聯合干預急跌後，匯價止跌回穩站上年線，有機會向上挑戰
月線及季線壓力。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：158.50-160.20 
前週交易區間 
157.60 159.56

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
         Aug/17/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週 EUR/USD 震盪走升。週初，歐元延續前一週創高回落的走勢，在美伊不
確定性再次升溫帶動避險需求下，EUR/USD弱勢整理；週間，市場在等待美國CPI
數據公布，交投清淡，歐元在1.1530到 1.1550之間窄幅震盪；臨近週末，美國經濟
數據顯示聯準會 9 月升息預期降溫，加上歐元區經濟面好轉，帶動歐元最高來到
1.1585，終場收在 1.1569，週線上漲 21點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏多震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐元區 8 月投資者信
心指數連續四個月走揚並創下今年 2 月以來新高，主要得益於市場對現況評估的顯
著好轉，顯示美伊戰爭引發的衝擊已逐漸被消化。同時，核心經濟體德國的信心指
數亦大幅回升，且第二季0.2%的微幅成長顯示整體經濟初顯企穩跡象。未來如果有
更多正面經濟數據將有效抵禦高昂能源成本所帶來的逆風，使歐元走勢有望轉為築
底反彈的偏多格局。二、美國 7 月 CPI 月增 0.1%、核心 CPI 年增 2.5%，主因汽油
價格連兩月下跌，顯示中東油價衝擊尚未引發通膨螺旋。通膨降溫疊加疲軟的就業
數據，促使市場對聯準會 9 月升息的預期大幅下降，短線上對歐元有利。三、技術
面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD匯價在多次挑戰 1.1540關鍵價位後終於有效突破，
有望壓力轉為支撐，短天期均線上彎且原先下彎的中長天期均線趨平，顯示有近一
步上漲的空間。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現區間偏多震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1500-1.1650 
前週交易區間 
1.1510 1.1585

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
新台幣利率走勢分析

115.08.10-115.08.14 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68%  
  公債－10年期- A15106 1.96~1.968% 
      － 5 年期- A15108     1.84%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，歷經上週較為密集的股利發放後，資金情勢稍有改善，不
過隨著股市持續創新高，產生部分吸金效應，短率依然持穩於近期區間高位。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.663%，略高於
市場預期區間 1.63%-1.66%，較上期標售結果上升 4.8個基本點。 
週五隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.830%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.67%~1.68%，利率低位較上週上揚約 2 個基本點。

二、 本週台債殖利率續於高檔， 10 年期指標券 A15106 搓合成交區間為
1.96%~1.968%，5年期指標券 A15108週四成交在 1.84%。 
週五 2 年期乙類公債得標利率為 1.725%，低於市場預期區間 1.75%~1.78%，
足額標售且投標倍數達 2.28 倍，顯示利率走升後吸引買盤進場，但在國內外
債市偏空的情況下，得標利率創 2007年以來新高。 
連續兩次標債足額標售，且得標利率符合或低於預期，有助於緩解台債殖利率
彈升壓力，不過美債利率仍處高位，加上台灣通膨持續高於2%，預期央行貨
幣政策將維持緊縮基調，短線上台債殖利率將隨美債走勢呈高檔盤整格局，預
估 5 年券交易區間在 1.82%~1.86%、10年券交易區間在 1.93%~1.98%。

2026 年9 月9 日 全 3 頁

2026 年 9月日銀短観予想 
AI・半導体需要で製造業は改善、非製造業はコスト増で悪化を予想

経済調査部 エコノミスト 中村 華奈子

[要約] 
 2026 年10 月1 日に公表予定の9 月日銀短観において、大企業製造業の業況判断DI（最
近）は＋25％pt（前回調査からの変化幅：＋3％pt）、同非製造業では＋34％pt（同：
▲3％pt）を予想する。 
 大企業製造業では、半導体・AI 関連需要の好調さなどを背景に、素材・加工業種とも
に景況感が改善するとみられる。 「化学」 「非鉄金属」 「電気機械」など多くの業種で業
況判断 DI（最近）の上昇を予想する。他方、 「自動車」など一部業種は低下を見込む。 
 大企業非製造業では、 幅広い業種で景況感の悪化を予想する。「小売」 「宿泊・飲食サ
ービス」などでは、仕入価格やエネルギーコストの増加などが、 「建設」 「不動産」で
は金利上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加などが、景況感を下押ししたとみている。 
 2026 年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、含む土地、ソフトウェアと研究開発投資額
は含まない）は前年度比＋7.9％と、前回調査からの上方修正を予想する。省力化投資
や更新投資に加え、能力増強や半導体関連投資の需要は引き続き強く、企業の設備投
資意欲は堅調さを維持するだろう。

図表 1：業況判断 DI の予想

変化幅 変化幅 変化幅
 大企業 製造業 17　 22　 5　 17　 25　 3　 26　 1　
非製造業 36　 37　 1　 28　 34　 ▲3　 32　 ▲2　
 中小企業 製造業 7　 9　 2　 2　 10　 1　 10　 0　
非製造業 16　 15　 ▲1　 8　 11　 ▲4　 9　 ▲2　
（注）先行き（予想）の変化幅は、業況判断DI（最近、予想）からの変化幅。
（出所）日本銀行統計より大和総研作成

2 / 3

【業況判断 DI（最近） 】AI需要で製造業は改善/コスト増で非製は悪化を予想 
2026 年 10 月 1 日に公表予定の 9 月日銀短観において、大企業製造業の業況判断DI（最近）
は＋25％pt（前回調査からの変化幅：＋3％pt）、同非製造業では＋34％pt（同：▲3％pt）を
予想する（図表1）。 
製造業では、半導体・AI 関連需要の好調さを背景に、素材・加工業種ともに景況感の改善を
予想する。他方、非製造業では、中東情勢を背景としたコスト増の継続に加え、6 月の利上げ
の影響もあり資金調達コストの増加が景況感の重石となったとみている。 
大企業製造業のうち「素材業種」では、半導体・AI関連需要の拡大や価格転嫁の進展を背景
に「化学」や「非鉄金属」で業況判断 DI（最近）の上昇を見込む。 「繊維」や「紙・パルプ」
でも景況感の改善を予想する。他方、 「窯業・土石製品」ではエネルギーコストの増大が収益
を圧迫し、景況感を下押ししたとみている。 「加工業種」では、AI・半導体関連需要を受けて
「電気機械」で業況判断 DI（最近）の上昇を見込む。また「食料品」では、原材料価格や物流
費が上昇する中でも価格転嫁の進展や底堅い需要を背景に、景況感が改善したと予想する。他
方、輸出台数や国内販売台数の伸び悩みから、 「自動車」は業況判断DI（最近）の低下を見込
む。 
大企業非製造業では、 「小売」や「対個人サービス」、 「宿泊・飲食サービス」などで景況感
の悪化を予想する。中東情勢を背景とした仕入価格やエネルギーコストの増加が収益を一段と
圧迫したほか、物価高を背景とした節約志向が販売の重石となったとみている。また、 「建設」
や「不動産」でも業況判断 DI（最近）の低下を見込む。労務費や建設資材価格の上昇に加えて、
6 月の利上げの影響もあり、金利上昇に伴う資金調達コストの増加が事業環境の重石となった
とみられる。

【業況判断 DI（先行き） 】製造業の改善、非製造業の悪化は継続する見込み 
9 月日銀短観では、大企業製造業の業況判断 DI（先行き）は＋26％pt（最近からの変化幅：
＋1％pt）、同非製造業は＋32％pt（同：▲2％pt）を予想する（前掲図表 1） 。 
大企業製造業では、AI・半導体関連需要が引き続き企業の景況感を押し上げるとみている。
加工業種では「電気機械」や「はん用機械」 、 「金属製品」などで業況判断DI（先行き）の上昇
を見込むほか、 「食料品」でも価格転嫁の進展などを背景に改善を予想する。素材業種では、
「石油・石炭製品」や「窯業・土石製品」 、 「繊維」などの業種で景況感の改善を見込む。大企
業非製造業では、各種コストの高止まりや金利上昇観測 1が重石となり、幅広い業種で業況判
断 DI（先行き）が低下するとみられる。 「建設」や「不動産」で業況判断DI（先行き）の低下
を見込むほか、 「小売」や「運輸・郵便」などの業種でも、先行きの悪化を予想する。

【設備投資計画】2026 年度計画：前年度比＋7.9％と予想 
2026 年度の設備投資計画（全規模全産業、含む土地、ソフトウェアと研究開発投資額は含ま
ない）は前年度比＋7.9％と、前回調査からの上方修正を予想する（図表 3） 。人手不足対応の
ための省力化投資や更新投資に加え、能力増強や半導体関連投資の需要が引き続き強く、企業
の設備投資意欲は堅調さを維持していると見込まれる。中東情勢の悪化等に伴う原材料価格の
高騰は企業の投資判断を慎重化させ得るが、価格転嫁の進展から、コスト増による収益への影
響は限定的なものにとどまるだろう（図表4） 。

Document 174: 9月加息，美联储最不坏的选择？

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月加息，美联储最不坏的选择？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-09
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

⾮农数据爆表推升美联储 9 ⽉加息概率⾄ 60% ，沃什深陷 " 辜负市场 " 还是 " 辜负特朗普 " 的两
难。申银万国警告， 2015 年以来加息预期超 40% 从未落空，并且市场⾼加息预期在 CPI 数
据公布后⼤概率难以消退。若美联储此次破例，期限溢价恐遭反噬。但若加息落地且路径
不⼤幅上修，冲击或⽐市场预期温和得多。
摘要：
超预期⾮农数据将美联储 9 ⽉加息概率推⾄ 60% ，令美联储主席沃什陷⼊ " 辜负市场 " 还是 " 辜负特
朗普 " 的两难困境。
申银万国研究在 9 ⽉ 8 ⽇的最新报告中指出，历史规律表明，⼀旦市场加息预期超过 40% ，加息
从未落空；若此次预期落空，期限溢价或将⼤幅上⾏，市场⾯临 “ 反噬 ” ⻛险。
9 ⽉ 4 ⽇公布的 8 ⽉⾮农就业数据显⽰新增就业 16.2 万⼈，远超市场预期的 5.5 万⼈，直接触发市
场对美联储 9 ⽉加息预期的剧烈重定价。与此同时，油价反弹与 AI 通胀粘性令 8 ⽉ CPI ⼤幅回落的
概率仅约 10.6% ，这意味着市场的⾼加息预期在 CPI 数据公布后⼤概率难以消退。

在此背景下，申银万国认为， 9 ⽉加息或是美联储当前 " 最不坏的选择 "—— 不加息的代价可能是
期限溢价上⾏与市场反噬，⽽加息落地若未带来加息路径的⼤幅上修，对市场的冲击则相对有
限。
⾮农超预期，加息预期居⾼难下
8 ⽉⾮农数据公布后，市场对美联储 9 ⽉加息的定价迅速升温。 9 ⽉ 3 ⽇，美联储理事 Christopher
Waller 发表讲话后， 9 ⽉加息概率⼀度跌⾄ 50% ；但在⾮农数据公布后，概率重新回升⾄ 60% 左
右。
8 ⽉ CPI 数据，将成为 9 ⽉议息会议前最后⼀个关键变量。历史回溯显⽰，只有显著低于预期的
CPI 数据，才能带来市场加息预期的⼤幅下修。据申银万国统计， 2015 年以来，在通胀低预期情
况下， CPI 发布当⽇市场对下次会议的加息预期平均仅下修 6 个百分点；历史上仅出现过 13 次
CPI 发布后加息预期当⽇下修超过 10 个百分点的案例，其中仅 3 次发⽣在通胀持平或略超预期的
背景下，且均伴随疫情冲击、联储官员意外放鸽或银⾏业危机等外部冲击。

当前， 8 ⽉ CPI ⾯临能源与结构性通胀的双重压⼒。美伊冲突升级导致霍尔⽊兹海峡通⾏受阻，
油价震荡⾛⾼，美国湾区裂解价差升⾄ 67.9 美元 / 桶； AI 相关服务价格亦呈现结构性涨价趋势。
申银万国基于 4 项机构预测进⾏ 10000 次蒙特卡洛模拟，结果显⽰ CPI 显著低预期的概率仅约
10.6% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

40% 以上预期从未落空，落空或致期限溢价 " 反噬 "
当前美联储内部分歧极⼤。 7 ⽉议息会议出现 9 ⽐ 3 的投票结果后，内部分歧进⼀步加剧。从近期
表态来看， Beth Hammack 、 Neel Kashkari 、 Lorie Logan 三⼈相对偏鹰，持续呼吁加息；
Christopher Waller 、 John Williams 等⼈相对偏鸽；⽽沃什本⼈在 8 ⽉ 28 ⽇表⽰，若核⼼通胀未
明显改善则 " 还有⼯作要做 " ，已出现鹰派转向迹象。
历史数据对沃什的决策具有重要参考意义。据申银万国统计，2015 年以来的 92 次联邦议息会议
中，凡是在会议召开前 10 个交易⽇内市场加息预期超过 40% ，加息从未落空，共计 20 次均如期
或超预期落地。加息预期介于 30% ⾄ 40% 之间但最终落空的仅有 5 次，分别发⽣在 2015 年 9 ⽉、
2016 年 9 ⽉、 2018 年 5 ⽉、 2018 年 11 ⽉以及 2026 年 7 ⽉。
这 5 次落空案例中， 2015 年和 2016 年的情形因全球⻛险与疲软经济数据⽽获市场接受，但 2018

年 5 ⽉与 2026 年 7 ⽉的案例尤为值得警惕 —— 这两次分别是鲍威尔与沃什上任之初框架确⽴阶段
的会议，加息预期落空后均出现了⻓端期限溢价⼤幅上⾏的 " 反噬 " ：加息落空后 10 个交易⽇，
10 年期期限溢价分别上⾏ 5.0 个基点和 6.2 个基点。
特朗普的政治压⼒同样不可忽视。截⾄ 9 ⽉ 3 ⽇， Polymarket 数据显⽰⺠主党夺取参议院控制权
的概率为 51% ，⺠主党与共和党预测席位均为 50 席，特朗普⾯临巨⼤的中期选举失利压⼒。然
⽽，申银万国指出，历史回溯显⽰， 1983 年以来在中期选举年或在任总统寻求连任年的 9 ⽉，加
息落地共出现过 3 次，频率并不低于 " ⾮政治敏感年 " ； 2018 年，刚受特朗普提名上任的鲍威尔亦
顶住政治压⼒连续加息。综合来看，市场压⼒或使沃什的天平向加息侧倾斜。
加息落地冲击有限，关键在于路径是否上修
若 9 ⽉加息如期落地，历史规律显⽰其对资产价格的冲击持续性相对有限。据申银万国回溯 1990
年以来 51 次加息后的资产表现：美股通常呈现短期回调、中期修复的规律，结构上周期股表现

相对较弱； 10 年期美债收益率震荡⾛⾼，但期限溢价明显回落。
加息后资产⾛势的分化，主要取决于两个因素：加息是否超预期，以及加息落地后远期路径是
否上修。以 10 年期美债为例，在加息超预期落地的情况下，后 20 个交易⽇美债利率平均回落 9 个
基点；反⽽在加息幅度不及预期时，利率平均上⾏ 28 个基点。在远期加息路径明显上移的情况
下， 10 年期美债收益率后 20 个交易⽇平均上⾏ 35 个基点；⽽加息路径基本持平时，收益率平均
回落 5 个基点。
申银万国认为，若 9 ⽉加息略超预期落地，市场或将其视作未来⼀年加息的相对前置，未必带来
加息路径的⼤幅上修。理由在于：⼀⽅⾯， 8 ⽉⾮农数据受季调因素扰动较⼤，考虑到低招聘
率、低裁员率及低劳动参与率，美国就业市场仍维持 " 弱平衡 " ；另⼀⽅⾯，薪资增速未现明显上

Topics  China's consumption-boosting measures lack potency

The Chinese government has formulated its first five -year plan dedicated exclusively to expanding consumption. 
However, a rapid recovery from the real-estate slump and anxiety surrounding employment, particularly among young 
people, which lie at the root of consumers' growing preference for saving money, appears unlikely. Achieving the plan's 
goals is therefore expected to prove difficult.

■ Services consumption is positioned as top priority  
In July, the Chinese government formulated a 
plan to expand consumption during the 15th Five-
Year Plan period. This is the first time China has 
released a five -year plan dedicated exclusively to 
consumption. The plan's overarching goal is to 
increase the s hare of household consumption, 
including consumption of a broad range of 
services, in the overall economy. The aim is to 
transform China's economic growth model. 
China's economy has traditionally relied heavily 
on investment -led growth, and household 
consumption accounts for less than 40% of GDP, 
significantly below the 50 –60% seen in major 
European countries and Japan and the figure of 
almost 70% in the U.S. Meanwhile, with the real -
estate slump and excess production capacity 
becoming increasingly serious problems, the 
traditional investment-oriented growth model has 
reached its limits, making a shift to consumption -
driven growth an urgent priority. 
As a specific numerical target, the plan calls for 
raising total retail sales of consumer goods (goods 
retail + catering industry income) to around 60 trillion yuan by 2030. Achieving this target will require 
consumption to expand at an average annual rate of 3.7% over the five-year period. Unlike the overarching 
goal described above, however, this numerica l target does not include consumption of services other than 
the income of the catering industry.  
The plan presents six "crucial areas" for advancing initiatives to expand consumption: 1) qualitatively 
enhancing services consumption, 2) expanding and upgrading goods consumption, 3) fostering new 
consumption forms, models, and scenarios, 4) raising cons umption capacity, 5) optimizing the overall 
consumer environment, and 6) enhancing platforms and strengthening support mechanisms to boost 
consumption. Regarding services consumption, which is positioned at the top of this list, policies include 
supporting consumption in elderly care (silver economy) and childcare services, developing cultural and 
tourism consumption, promoting consumption in health and sports, and enhancing education and training 
services. At the meeting of the CCP Politburo at the end of July, the need to unlock the potential of services 
consumption was also emphasized, suggesting that the focus of the Chinese government's consumption -
boosting measures is shifting from goods to services consumption. 
■ Services consumption is being affected by keenness to save money 
In conjunction with the formulation of the plan, in June the government began publishing a new indicator 
designed to capture trends in consumption of a broad range of services: total retail sales of consumer goods 
and services. In addition to the income of  the catering industry, which was captured by the previous retail 
sales indicator (total retail sales of consumer goods), this new metric includes consumption of various other 
services, such as communication/information services, tourism/culture, and sports/leisure. A comparison of 
recent trends in the old and new indicators shows that while growth in the old indicator has continued to 
slow since 2024, the new indicator is relatively solid. 
Raising total retail sales of consumer goods to 
around 60 trillion yuan
1. Qualitatively enhancing services consumption 
2. Expanding and upgrading goods consumption 
3. Fostering new consumption forms, models, and 
scenarios 
4. Raising consumption capacity 
5. Optimizing the overall consumer environment 
6. Enhancing platforms and strengthening support 
mechanisms to boost consumption

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  September 2026, No.306

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

2 
Overall services consumption is therefore firm in 
comparison to goods consumption, though it still lacks 
the strength to become a driver of overall consumption. 
Looking at the breakdown of services consumption, 
spending patterns have diverged amid the econ omic 
slowdown since 2025, when the gap between the old 
and new indicators widened, as consumers have 
increasingly sought to save money.  Expenditure on 
items for which it is difficult to cut spending, such as 
medical/healthcare, transportation/communicatio n, and 
education, has tended to increase faster than disposable 
income. In contrast, spending on services that can be 
substituted with alternatives within the household, such 
as catering and domestic help, has stagnated. Housing -
related expenses, including  rent, have also remained 
weak against the backdrop of the real -estate slump. 
Furthermore, even tourism consumption, which the 
government has positioned as a pillar of consumption 
expansion, has been affected by economic considerations. Travelers are incre asingly favoring low -cost, 
close-to-home, and short-duration trips, with domestic travel more popular than overseas travel. Per capita 
spending by domestic tourists has also declined.  
■ Lack of measures to overcome structural weakness in consumption makes target difficult to 
achieve 
Among the other crucial areas, 3) fostering new consumption forms, models, and scenarios is expected 
to generate some additional demand through measures such as developing digital consumption by 
integrating artificial intelligence with consumption, promoti ng green (eco -friendly) consumption, and 
developing the "first-launch economy" in line with local conditions. The first -launch economy is a catch -
all term for economic activities involving "firsts," such as companies launching new products and 
introducing new business formats, technologies, and services, and overseas and domestic brands opening 
their first stores. 
However, a full -fledged recovery in consumption appears unlikely. The root causes of consumers' 
preference for saving are the negative wealth effect of the real -estate slump and a soft employment 
environment, particularly for young people. Although cities have introduced measures such as easing 
requirements for home purchases, structural factors, including a decline in the pool of potential home buyers, 
continue to weigh on housing sales. Prices of both new homes and used homes, which tend to better reflect

actual market conditions, have continued to decline, and inventory turnover periods also appear to be 
getting longer. A full-fledged recovery in the housing market is therefore unlikely to happen for a few more 
years. The employment situation also shows little sign of improvement. Looking at unemployment rates by 
age group since the beginning of this year, rates for those aged 16–24 and 30–59 have remained at the high 
levels seen before 2025, while the rate for those aged 25 –29 has deteriorated to above las t year's level. 
Consumer confidence, which has been in "pessimistic" territory since 2022 amid the real -estate downturn, 
has worsened further since the start of this year. 
The five-year plan mentions such measures as promoting high -quality and full employment, optimizing 
the supply of affordable housing, and increasing asset income via various channels, such as by improving 
the health of the real-estate market. However, it does not set out specific policies. In addition, advances in 
artificial intelligence could lead to job displacement, causing a further heightening of household anxiety 
about the future, so paradoxically, the technology could actually worsen consumer confidence. Because the 
five-year plan does not adequately address the root causes of weak consumption, the transition to a 
consumption-driven economy is expected to prove challenging.

Topics  AI boom is stoking inflation concerns in South Korea

Rising semiconductor prices are spilling over into durable goods prices in South Korea. In addition, widening demands 
for "N% performance-based bonuses," which originated in the semiconductor industry and involve a certain percentage 
of the company's profit being returned to employees in the form of bonuses, could lead to broader-based price increases.

■ Semiconductor prices soar amid the AI boom  
Against the backdrop of the worldwide AI boom, 
prices of memory chips, in which South Korean 
companies hold a large share of the global market, 
have surged. Price increases are spreading beyond 
advanced memory chips for AI data centers to 
general-purpose memory chips used in a wide range 
of digital devices, including PCs and smartphones. 
Semiconductor manufacturers have shifted 
production capacity toward more profitable 
advanced products, resulting in shortages of general-
purpose products and pushing up their prices. 
Such supply -demand pressures are likely to 
persist for the time being. In South Korea's 
semiconductor industry, fabs are operating at high 
utilization rates, leaving limited scope for increasing 
output at existing facilities. Meeting the brisk 
demand thus  necessitates a build -out of new 
production capacity. In response, major South 
Korean chipmakers Samsung Electronics and SK 
hynix are planning large-scale investments, but a substantial expansion in supply is not expected until 2027 
at the earliest. Building large-scale fabs and putting them into operation takes considerable time, and even 
after a facility begins operating, it typic ally takes several months to a year to complete adjustments to 
improve yields and establish mass-production capabilities. There will therefore be a significant wait before 
increased production capacity leads to price stabilization. 
■ Wave of price increases spreads to semiconductor-containing products 
The surge in chip prices has already spilled over 
into prices of finished goods. Price increases have 
been particularly noticeable with products that 
contain large numbers of memory chips, such as 
PCs, smartphones, game consoles, and storage 
devices. Prices of these durable goods had generally 
been on a downward trend over the past 20 years, 
reflecting improvements in performance driven by 
technological advances as well as declining 
production costs. Recently, however, they have 
turned upward, pushing up co nsumer prices. PC 
prices, in particular, have risen markedly, increasing 
by approximately 20% between January and July 
this year.   
Going forward, inflationary pressure is expected 
to remain strong, particularly for durable goods. In 
the case of products containing large quantities of 
semiconductors, manufacturers are likely to 
continue passing higher memory chip costs through 
to selling prices. Furthermore, such developments are not limited to digital devices. The wave of price 
increases could spread to automobiles and smart home appliances, which also contain numerous chips. In 
the auto industry, in particular, the increasing sophistication of vehicles has led to greater semiconductor 
usage, making it likely that soaring manufacturing costs will be passed on to consumers.

5 
■ Demands for "N% performance-based bonuses" are spreading across South Korea 
At South Korean semiconductor giant 
SK hynix, employees received 
exceptionally large performance -based 
bonuses in February this year, with the 
payouts exceeding their annual salaries. 
This was attributable not only to a sharp 
increase in the company's ear nings, 
which were boosted by the global AI 
boom, but also to a revamp last year of 
the firm's bonus system, whereby 10% of 
operating profit would be allocated to a 
pool for performance -based bonuses. In 
May, following SK hynix's enormous 
bonus payout, the labor union at Samsung 
Electronics threatened a strike unless 
employees received performance -based 
bonuses on a similar scale. Although the 
strike was ultimately avoided, labor and 
management in the semiconductor 
division agreed to allocate 10.5% of the 
business's profit to special bonuses. 
Estimates based on this agreement and 
earnings forecasts for this year indicate 
that the average performance -based 
bonus to be paid next year by the 
semiconductor divisions of SK hynix and Samsung Electronics will exceed 50 million yen per employee.   
Demands for "N% performance -based bonuses," whereby a certain percentage of profit is returned to 
employees, are spreading beyond the semiconductor industry into other sectors, too. In manufacturing, 
labor unions in the automobile, heavy industry, non-ferrous metals, and pharmaceutical sectors are calling 
for a certain percentage of operating or net profit to be allocated to performance -based bonuses, and in the 
service sector, unions in segments such as information and communications are making similar dem ands. 
At major IT company Kakao, for example, the labor union launched a strike after demanding that 13 –15% 
of operating profit be allocated to a special bonus pool. Later, following 18 rounds of wage negotiations, 
the union and management reached an agree ment entailing a substantial increase in annual salaries. 
Meanwhile, the labor union at Naver, another IT firm, secured a significant hike in base pay relatively 
quickly by refraining from demanding N% performance-based bonuses. 
■ Rising labor costs could lead to higher prices 
If such trends spread, they could push up wage levels across a broad range of industries, rather than 
simply resulting in temporary increases in performance -based bonuses at highly profitable companies. 
While firms remain cautious about introducing N% perf ormance-based bonuses, labor unions are 
leveraging calls for such schemes in wage 
negotiations, and as noted above, there have already 
been cases in which negotiations surrounding 
performance-based bonuses have resulted in increases 
in base pay. If the high levels of compensation paid in 
the semiconductor and IT industries are emulated by 
other sectors, upward pressure on wages could spread 
more broadly. This is particularly likely in industries 
where competition among companies to attract talent 
is intense, as the firms are conscious of wage increases 
by other companies and tend to follow suit to stay 
competitive in the job market. If such wage 
competition continues, wage hikes at highly profitable 
companies could lead to similar moves at firms whose 
earnings environment is far less favorable.

There is a danger of widespread wage rises intensifying inflationary pressure by pushing service prices 
higher. Wage increases that are not accompanied by productivity gains squeeze corporate earnings as they 
push up labor costs, which creates an incentive for com panies to raise selling prices. This is particularly 
significant in South Korea's service sector, where labor productivity growth has remained sluggish for many 
years, leaving companies with limited scope to absorb wage increases through productivity gains . Indeed, 
in previous periods of rising wages in the service sector, service price inflation has tended to accelerate, so 
the situation will need to be watched closely. 
(Thoma Imagawa)

Document 207: Central Bank Credibility as a risk_ Why the ECB leads, the BoJ lags and the Fed and BoE are scrutinized

Source PDF: all report pdf/Central Bank Credibility as a risk_ Why the ECB leads, the BoJ lags and the Fed and BoE are scrutinized.pdf
Report source: Allianz Trade
Date: 2026-09-02
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

In Summary

Central bank credibility is becoming a risk rather than a background condition.  
At 
Jackson Hole
 , 
the new Fed Chairman 
 called for a quieter central bank, breaking with 
two decades of managing expectations while  
the 
 US Treasury is co
 -
steering financial 
conditions through debt maturity and buybacks. Meanwhile in Europe, cancelling debt 
held by the Eurosystem has entered the French presidential campaign and investors still 
remember the Bank of England's emergency bond p
 urchases during the Mini
 -
Budget 
crisis. Against geopolitical tension, partisan polarization, inflation overshooting and 
elevated sovereign debt, these are all risks for central bank credibility  
– 
long a shock 
absorber for markets, it 
 may  
start amplifying shocks instead.

We introduce a framework measuring credibility on six dimensions: the ECB tops 
the ranking, the BoJ carries the highest overall risk, and the Fed and BoE each show 
different vulnerabilit
 ies
. 
 We use six dimensions, three are structural
 : institutional 
credibility  
 (legal independence, leadership stability), 
 expectation credibility  
 (how 
memory of past inflation shapes expectations) and the 
 credibility gap  
 (control over 
long
 -
term 
yield trend
 ); and three are cyclical: 
 credibility risk sentiment  
(media coverage 
questioning independence), 
 market credibility  
(how well prices and surveys track the 
inflation target) and 
 credibility pressure  
(difficulty defending  
the price of 
 mone
y
). The 
ECB ranks highest, an asset for the euro's international role. The Fed is strong 
structurally but weaker cyclically; the BoE's weakness is structural. The BoJ carries the 
highest risk, hit by 
 JPY  
depreciation  
and long
 -
end steepening at once.

Two trends capture the erosion: news
 -
based concern is at its worst since 2017, 
and central banks are judged on a shorter track record than before 2022.  
The Fed 
drives 70% of global news coverage 
 on 
central
 -
bank credibility, yet Americans are less 
worried than the rest of the world. The opposite holds for the ECB, BoJ and BoE, where 
concern is mainly domestic. In parallel
 , 
we estimate how many years of past inflatio
 n 
shape expectations 
 – 
how long the public's memory of past mistakes lasts. That memory 
fell from eight years to four in the 1980s disinflation fight, rebuilt to an 8
 -
12
-
year peak 
by 2021, then was cut toward early
 -
2000s levels by 2022. Credibility holds on market 
and survey measures, but long
 -
earned trust has been in part lost.

Central banks have not regained their grip o
 n the  
 long
 -
term yield trend after 
ending QE, opening a credibility gap. 
 For the Fed and BoJ, yields sit above what policy 
implies 
 – 
closing that gap 
 may 
 require buying long
 -
term bonds again. For the ECB and 
BoE th
 is 
is less urgent. Each 
 gap 
 opened at a distinct balance
 -
sheet turn: end of QE for 
the Fed, end of yield
 -
curve control for the BoJ, start of QE for the ECB and BoE. Markets 
seem to underprice renewed bond
 -
buying odds in the US and Japan.

Restoring credibility means acting on four fronts, the cheapest being the most 
urgent:  
 publish a rule
 -
based framework with voting records so giving up forward 
guidance reads as discipline, not silence; anchor communication in reference points like 
wage growth or nominal GDP; define the Treasury relationship before issuance 
becomes a rival l
 ever on rates and, most urgently, decide now how to respond if rates 
hit zero again, rather than improvise under the next shock. Credibility once let central 
banks avoid intervening at all; reversed, it will amplify shocks instead of absorbing them 
–
and the Fed and BoJ have the least room to disappoint.

Central bank credibility 
 moved from

 to

To establish which of the G4  
(Fed, ECB, BoJ, and BoE)  
is most exposed  
to credibility risk
 , we 
 built

a 
f

combining six 
 measures
 . O
n the structural side we analyze

 As cyclical measure we propose the 
 news sentiment (Credibility Risk Sentiment),

 and 
 the price of money (Credibility Pressure)
 .  
We focus on the price stability target and policy rate setting, which the four share, recognizing that some

The institutional framework: between 
 de jure  
and 
 de facto  
independence

Legal central bank independence is at a peak globally, but is mainly an European phenomenon.  
F
rom a 
legal point of view, their independence has never been stronger. Over the last 50 years,  
this independence 
has been increasingly enshrined in the constitutional framework of developed and emerging countries. 
 T
he 
main push did not come with the monetarist revolution of the 1980s. While the subordination of monetary to 
fiscal policy was identified at the time as a reason for the ineffectiveness of central banks during the 1970s 
inflation shock, this did not lead 
 to a legal (
 de jure
 ) separation of political and monetary decision
 -
making. 
Instead, the independence of monetary policy came about through a targeted board appointment policy, 
which led to a change in economic culture and, ultimately, to a form of informal 
 de facto  
institutionalization. 
The biggest global push in 
 de jure  
independence came in the 1990s with the creation of the Eurozone, when 
future member countries adjusted to the standard of the German Bundesbank. One could even say that 
elevated 
 de jure  
 central bank 
 independence is a largely European phenomenon. The US, UK, Japan, 
Australia and most emerging  
countries have only slightly tightened their legal frameworks over time. Their 
independence mainly relies on 
 de facto  
elements. Among the G4 central banks, institutional credibility is 
weakest for the 
 BoJ  
and 
 BoE 
 (Figures 1 & 2).

of central

The ECB and the Fed contain market

 and survey

 The conventional approach to

along
 side survey

 s

 ’

Taken alone, n
 either market prices nor surveys measure the expectations that bear on central
 -
bank 
credibility.  
Market
 -
implied measures are prices, not beliefs: risk and liquidity premia, hedging demand and 
balance
 -
sheet constraints can displace them by tens of basis points without any revision of expectations. The 
distortion is largest especially  
 under stress, when the reading matters most.
 2  
They are also subject to 
Goodhart's law 
 – 
once agents learn that a central bank reacts to a particular price, that price begins to reflect 
the anticipated reaction function, rather than belief.
 3 
Surveys extend coverage to households and price
 -
setting firms, but at the cost of a very short effective memory, which leaves them unable to separate a 
transitory relative
 -
price shock from an erosion of the nominal anchor.
 4 
We therefore complete them in two 
steps. First, we redirect market data from the level of expected inflation to regime and intermediation signals:

2 
Distortions arise from inflation risk and liquidity premia, regulation
 -
driven hedging of real liabilities, central bank 
balance
 -
sheet operations and for OTC inflation swaps counterparty credit and the funding cost of collateral. One 
illustration: the euro  
5y5y fell to roughly 0.75% in March 2020 at the height of the dealer
 -
intermediation dislocation. See 
D'Amico, Kim and Wei (2018); Fleckenstein, Longstaff and Lustig (2014)  
3 
The euro 5y5y is the clearest case. 
 Mario 
Draghi elevated it to a quasi
 -
official credibility gauge at Jackson Hole in August 
2014; after its failure in 2020
 ,

our 
 Credibility Pressure
 . Second, we model expectation formation as an adaptive process with an error
 -
dependent estimation window: our 
 Expectation Credibility
 .
5

Central
 -
bank credibility pressure has normalized, but pockets of instability remain

Instead of naively deriving inflation expectations from market prices, we define price stability as the 
central bank capacity to preserve the value of money under stress
 . 
We define price stability as the central 
bank's capacity to preserve the value of money, and measure the pressure on it across Mehrling's four prices 
of money.  
6

The aggregate is our 
 Credibility Pressure  
indicator. It reads the regime a central bank operates 
in, rather than the level of any inflation price.

1. 
 The Par Value:  
This 
is defined as the law of one price between private money and public money. We 
proxy it using the spread between the 
 three
-
month Treasury bill yield, representing public money, 
and the 
 three
-
month OIS rate, representing the risk
 -
free price of bank
 -
created money. Any widening 
of this spread signals tensions within the monetary system and growing doubts about the central 
bank's ability to keep short
 -
term rates anchored. This measure has gained 
 importance with the 
emergence of the 
 Activist Treasury Issuance  
 (ATI) in the US. Heavy bills issuance represents an 
expansion of public money supply from the Treasury, rather than the monetary authority. While the 
Fed's reverse repo facility (RRP) carried a large balance, that supply was absorbed without a price 
effec
t. With the facility drained, incremental bill issuance has to be taken down by real money, and 
the price appears in the Bill 
 - 
OIS spread. The marginal price of the safest short rate is now influenced 
by the Treasury, not the central bank alone. This coul
 d lead to fiscal dominance visible at the front 
end. The 
 Par Value  
would be the earliest to register it. Currently, the 
 Par Value  
indicates no danger 
of front end fiscal dominance, but the US and UK are structurally more inclined to it  (Figure 13).

2. 
 The Price of Goods:  
We measure 
 this 
as the spread between inflation rate and the overnight risk
 -
free rate. This spread indicates whether inflation is running above or below the effective monetary 
stance. A widening positive gap suggests increasing pressure on the central bank's ability to 
m
aintain price stability and re
 -
anchor inflation expectations.

3. 
 The Foreign Price of Money:  
 This 
 is defined by the Real Effective Exchange Rate (REER)
 , which  
captures the inflation
 -
adjusted value of a currency against a basket of trading partners. It measures 
the external purchasing power and competitiveness of a currency. Sustained deviations from its 
historical equilibrium can signal growing doubts about the  
 central bank's ability to preserve the 
external value of money.  
4. 
 The Future Price of Money
 : We proxy 
 this 
through the steepening of the long end of the yield curve, 
measured by the 5y30y term spread. When the long end rises significantly relative to intermediate 
maturities, investors may be questioning the central bank's ability to control long
 -
term rates and  
risk 
premia, thereby putting pressure on its credibility.

During the post
 -
pandemic inflation shock, the Fed, the ECB and the BoE experienced significant credibility 
pressure.  
For all three, the pressure originated primarily from the 
 Price of Goods  
(inflation) and 
 Future Price 
of Money  
(curve steepness) dimensions of money. Following the aggressive monetary tightening since 2023, 
credibility pressure has eased and almost returned to neutral territory by now. Pockets of pressure remain in 
the steepening of the long end of the yield curve
 . (Figure 12).

5 
Consistent with the experience
 -
weighting evidence, see Malmendier and Nagel (2016) and adjacent to endogenous
 -
gain learning, see Orphanides and Williams (2005)  
6 
Mehrling, P. (2023)

### Page 7

For the BoJ, the situation looks different.  
Having delayed 
 the 
monetary response to inflation pressures, its 
credibility pressure has mounted across three dimensions simultaneously: JPY depreciation has weakened 
the foreign price of money, inflation overshoot affects the price of goods and the steepening of the long e
 nd 
of the yield curve suggests growing uncertainty regarding the future value of money
 . 
While tensions in the 
domestic monetary system remain relatively contained, the Bank of Japan's credibility is today the weakest 
in this framework. (Figure 14).

More broadly, credibility pressure rarely emerges uniformly across all dimensions.  
Instead, it typically 
originates in one dimension before spreading to the others. By identifying the initial source of pressure and 
monitoring its transmission, 
 Credibility Pressure  
 provides a dynamic measure of effective central
 -
bank 
credibility rather than monitoring level of inflation prices. It can also help to detect early signs of potential 
policy constraints before they become visible through conventional indicators

### Page 8

The broken of stability of monetary policy e
 xpectations   
Monetary policy expectations have entered a fragile consolidation after the post
 -
Covid inflation shock.  
Since policy operates through expectations, credibility has to be measured through them instead of solely 
relying on noisy market
 -
implied and survey
 -
based indicators. We construct expectations as a weighted 
average of past observations in which the rate o
 f forgetting rises with the magnitude of recent forecast errors: 
memory contracts under surprise and lengthens with accuracy.
 7 
The estimation window that results is the 
credibility measure. It reads as how far back agents still find the past informative. An eight
 -
year window 
means expectations rest on roughly the last eight years of data before memory decays materially, a four
 -
ye
ar window means that everything before the last cycle has been discarded. Two structural features stand 
out. Inflation expectations are consistently formed over longer horizons than policy rate expectations: agents 
trust the inflation regime further out th
 an they trust the reaction function. And credibility hit a global low in 
the early 1980s, when surging inflation and a break in the policy response cut the average window from eight 
years to four. Through the Great Moderation, central banks rebuilt it stea
 dily, uninterrupted by the GFC. The 
peak came in 2021: 
 eight  
years for inflation expectations in the UK and US, 
 nine 
in the Eurozone, 12 in Japan; 
seven 
years for policy rates at the BoE, Fed and ECB, again 12 at the BoJ. The 2022 inflation shock broke that 
trend. Inflation memory shortened by up to two years, back to early
 -
2000s levels, and the adjustment in 
policy rate expectations was sharper still, par
 ticularly in the UK and US. The BoJ was the exception. 
Expectation credibility is now consolidating, but precariously. Another inflation or deflation shock would take 
it below anything seen since the 1980s (Figure 15).

The credibility gap: Central banks have not yet regained their grip on the yield curve  
The balance
 -
sheet grip has gone and rate decisions have not replaced it. 
 We measure credibility by the 
extent to which a central bank is able to effectively set the level of yields. To measure this "grip on the yield 
curve" we use a decomposition that splits cumulative yield changes into those realized around policy 
meetings (t
 -
2 to t+2) and those realized in between.
 8 
To capture the long
 -
term anchoring of yield levels, we 
use the 10
 -
year maturity. The more the meeting window determines the trend in actual long
 -
term yields, the

better the central bank is able to anchor rate expectations. The difference between the observed yield and 
the policy
 -
window trend is thus a simple estimate of a "credibility gap"  measuring how far the market has 
walked away from the central bank's anchor
 . For a credible central bank, the anchor holds between 
meetings and intermediate price moves are mostly offset. When the gap opens and stays open, policy 
meetings are unable to re
 -
anchor the trend and the market sets long
 -
term rates in response to other s
 ignals. 
For the US, between 2000 and 2008
 , 
the FOMC meeting window did a decent job of setting the long
 -
term 
trend. When the Zero Lower Bound was reached and QE was introduced, the grip on the yield curve moved 
from rate decisions to the balance sheet. Since QE ended, the Fed's credibility gap has  
switched signs and 
widened progressively. The evolution is similar for the BoJ, where the surge came with the end of yield curve 
control in 2024 and is still deteriorating rapidly. The ECB and the BoE show a wide, negative gap that also 
opened with the ZL
 B and QE. The credibility gap analysis shows that central banks find themselves in a place 
where the balance
 -
sheet grip has gone and has not yet been replaced by a grip through policy rate decisions 
(Figure 16).

The probability of a return to duration purchases, or of other measures to regain a grip on the curve, is 
higher in the US and Japan
 . One might object that this is not a loss of monetary grip, but a term premium 
rebuilding on fiscal supply and inflation risk. The timing argues otherwise. 
 Term premium repricing was a 
common movement that began in 2022 and ran across all four jurisdictions more or less simultaneously. The 
credibility gaps did not. Each opened at an important change in that central bank's own balance sheet 
regime (start of QE  
for the ECB and the BoE, end of QE for the Fed and end of yield
 -
curve control for the BoJ). 
A common shock does not produce staggered responses. The term premium is an enhancing factor, not the 
cause. 
 Today all four face similarly sized gaps but with different signs, positive for the Fed and BoJ, negative

for the ECB and BoE. Closing them requires either regaining influence over the trend through a credible and 
legible reaction function, or acting on the yield itself through the balance sheet. The sign determines the 
urgency. Where the gap is positive, as f
 or the Fed and the BoJ, the market is holding long yields above the 
policy
 -
implied trend and closing it means buying duration. Where the gap is negative, as for the ECB and the 
BoE, the pressure runs the other way and is less acute. The probability of a re
 turn to duration purchases, or 
of other measures to regain grip on the curve, is therefore materially higher in the US and Japan. Markets 
may be underpricing this. A further credibility risk would be if Treasury were to massively buy back long
 -

 -

ECB with highest credibility, 
 followed by the Fed. BoJ with highest credibility risk  
The ECB scores highest, followed by the Fed. The credibility risk is structural for the BoE, but cyclical for 
the Fed and the BoJ.  
When we apply our six measures of central
 -
bank credibility and establish an equally 
weighted ranking among the G4 central banks, the ECB scores equally well in structural and cyclical 
measures
 , 
emerging on top, while Fed credibility is strong structurally but weaker cyclically. The credibility 
of the ECB is an asset that could be used to strengthen the international role of the 
 e
uro.
9 
In comparison, the 
BoE shows an elevated credibility risk
 , 
suffering notably from weak scores in structural measures such as 
expectations, anchoring and institutional framework. The BoJ shows the highest credibility risk, suffering 
from weak scores in cyclical indicators such as credibility sentiment and pressure
 . This means that any 
premium that is priced for central
 -
bank credibility is more of 
 a 
cyclical nature for the BoJ and the Fed
 , and  
is 
more structural for the BoE (Table 1).

How to restore central bank credibility?

The w
 ithdrawal of forward guidance is not a credibility risk if communication is embedded in an “explain  
or comply
 ” framework.  
Central banks hold no informational advantage as public data usually forecast 
policy rates and inflation about as well as markets do.
 10 
Guidance is therefore always part forecast and part 
promise, and withdrawing forward guidance is not in itself a credibility loss. What a central bank does know 
better than the market is its own reaction function. Publishing a benchmark rule, explaining d
 eviations from 
it and releasing vote tallies as a gauge of how stable the rule is and how long a deviation is expected to last 
would be the genuine credibility enhancer.

Develop a more nominal framing of  
policy targets.  
Under inflation targeting, a supply shock forces a choice 
between tolerating a strong inflation overshoot and crushing output; under nominal GDP (NGDP) level 
targeting
 , 
 the framework answers for itself since the nominal target is left broadly unchanged. A more

nominal framing, including nominal wages, also makes communication legible to the wider public. But the 
switch is itself the credibility cost. The better route is to publish nominal wage growth and NGDP as reference 
variables against which decisions are ex
 plained, leaving the mandate untouched.

Prepare for the next credibility challenge
 . 
The recent strategic reviews adapted to a world of supply shocks: 
the Fed abandoned average inflation targeting, the BoJ abandoned its inflation
 -
overshooting commitment. 
Both frameworks were built for 
 a 
 persistent undershoot that failed its first supply shock. We see that as a 
sign that the prevailing economic culture in central banks, and the models that embed it, have shifted 
decisively towards inflation vigilance. Should deflationary pressure return (A
 I
-
driven supply expansion and 
productivity gains, demographics, Chinese excess capacity), the available instruments may prove neither 
adequate nor prompt. Strategic reviews on a fixed schedule tend to be procyclical and cap the horizon 
agents are willing t
 o believe in. The lower
 -
bound answer is best decided now, while it costs nothing, rather 
than at the bound.

Manage the relationship with the Treasury.  
This is m
 ainly a US question today, but potentially a broader 
one as debt
 -
service costs rise. If issuance composition can offset monetary policy, the policy rate stops 
summarizing the stance and forecasting monetary policy becomes a two
 -
player problem. For the Treasury, 
the remedy is a public recommitment to regular and predicta
 ble issuance. For the central bank, it is a stance 
measure that internalizes Treasury duration supply. Making ATI visible makes it less challenging for central 
bank credibility.

Document 211: Codex主管Tibo：“清晰的意图表达、架构品味与系统把控力”是AI时代最不可替代的

Source PDF: all report pdf/Codex主管Tibo：“清晰的意图表达、架构品味与系统把控力”是AI时代最不可替代的.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-10
Pages: 30
Status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI Codex 主管认为， AI 时代， “ 如果你⽆法解释你在试图实现什么，如果你⽆法表达
你的意图，如果你与你所服务的社区没有连接，如果你没有品味 —— 那做出好的⼯作会难
得多。 ” 他透露， OpenAI 已将 AI 代码审查模型强制应⽤于所有 PR ，安全检测全⾃动、 “ 超越
⼈类⽔平 ” 。
摘要：
当 AI 能写代码、做审查、跑重构，⼯程师的核⼼价值究竟剩下什么？
近⽇， OpenAI Codex 主管 Tibo Sottiaux 在 The Pragmatic Engineer 播客中表⽰，在 AI 快速接管
代码执⾏层的今天，⼯程师真正难以被替代的，是另⼀套能⼒。
这三件事 ——清晰的意图表达、架构品味、系统把控⼒—— 是他给所有⼯程师的核⼼建议，也
是他在 Codex 团队内部反复强调的⼯作原则。
在他看来， AI 时代真正稀缺的能⼒有两类：
第⼀，深度好奇⼼与快速理解系统的能⼒。 “ 在 OpenAI 做得好的⼈，都是那种能快速读懂⼀个
系统、钻进⼀个新代码库、迅速搞清楚它在做什么的⼈。 ” 他说，这种能⼒在有了 AI 辅助之后反⽽

更重要，因为需要吸收和推理的信息量更⼤了。
第⼆，清晰的意图表达与架构品味。 Tibo 强调，随着 AI 承担越来越多的执⾏⼯作，⼯程师需要
更清楚地定义 “ 盒⼦ ” 的边界 —— 它应该做什么、满⾜哪些不变量 —— ⽽不是纠结于盒⼦⾥⾯的实
现细节。 “ ⼀旦你们对盒⼦的功能和约束达成共识，盒⼦⾥⾯发⽣什么，其实不需要太在意。 ”
AI 代码审查已 “ 超越⼈类 ” ， OpenAI 全⾯强制执⾏
“ 如果你⽆法解释你在试图实现什么，如果你⽆法表达你的意图，如果你与你所服务的社区没有
连接，如果你没有品味 —— 那做出好的⼯作会难得多。 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Tibo 在节⽬中透露， Codex 团队早期就开发了专⻔的代码审查模型，能够深⼊追踪多层依赖关
系，发现⼈类审查员需要数⼩时才能定位的逻辑错误。
“ 这些模型在代码审查上已经达到超⼈⽔平 —— 不只是正确性，安全性也是。 ” 他说。⽬前，
OpenAI 所有 Pull Request 都强制经过 AI 安全扫描，⼀旦被标记安全问题， PR 将被阻⽌合并， “ 全
部⾃动化 ” 。
但 Tibo 认为，代码审查真正的价值从来不只是找 bug 。 “ 它⼀直是⼀种信息交换的仪式，让⼤家对
⻬，引发讨论 —— 理想情况下这些讨论应该在写代码之前就发⽣。 ”
他的判断是：正确性和安全性的审查将被⾃动化；⽽真正值得⼈类投⼊注意⼒的，是围绕意图
的讨论 ——“ 你到底想做什么？这件事值得做吗？ ” 这个讨论不必发⽣在 Pull Request ⾥，但必须
发⽣。
维护成本下降，架构能⼒反⽽更重要
Tibo 认为，维护成本的⼤幅下降，正在重塑⼯程师的价值分布。
更⼤的变化在于系统重构。 “ 过去，完整的架构重构可能需要数年。现在这个成本被极度压缩

了。 ” 但他同时强调，这反⽽让架构判断⼒变得更重要，⽽不是更不重要：
他提到， OpenAI 内部有时会在⼀个周末内，让 100 个 Agent 同时向同⼀个项⽬贡献代码。 “ 这种
规模的协作以前需要⼏年时间慢慢扩张，现在可能⼀个周末就发⽣了。 ”
为什么⽤ Rust ，为什么开源，为什么⽀持竞争对⼿的模型
Codex CLI ⽤ Rust 编写，在当时是⼀个反直觉的决定 —— 彼时模型在 Rust 上的能⼒远不如
Python 或 TypeScript 。
Tibo 的解释是： “ 我们从第⼀原则出发，把产品界⾯和 Agent 本⾝视为两件不同的事。 Agent 的核
⼼需要健壮、安全、⾼效。 ” 他承认⽤ TypeScript 也能成功， “ 但如果你把所有东西写在同⼀个代
码库⾥，你不可避免地会把东西耦合在⼀起，然后阻碍后续创新。 Rust 的边界在这⾥⾮常有⽤。 ”
开源的决定同样出于务实考量。 “ 你在构建⼀个编码 Agent ，当然要把它指向⾃⼰。 ”Tibo 说，开源
让社区参与成为可能，也让团队招聘变得更简单 ——“ 新⼈⼊职，他们已经看过代码库了，

onboarding 基本就完成了。 ”
⾄于⽀持其他模型提供商， Tibo 的逻辑更直接： “ 如果我们不⽀持，任何⼈都可以 fork ⼀份然后加
上 10 ⾏代码。那样只会让⽤⼾去⽤那个 fork ，毫⽆意义。 ” 他说， “ 我希望靠最好的模型、最⾼效
的模型、最好的产品赢得⽤⼾ —— ⽽不是靠锁定。 ”
维护本质上是你为了让系统持续运转⽽不得不⽀付的税。但现在，⼤量维护⼯作将被⾃动化 ——
⽐如第三⽅依赖升级，模型可以在⼏⼩时内扫完整个代码库，直接搞定。以前⼤家会拖着不做，
因为不好玩，但它对安全来说⾮常重要。
好的抽象、清晰的边界、明确的不变量 —— 如果你把形状画对了，你就能在 ' 盒⼦ ' 内部快速改变
任何东西，⽽不影响其他服务。要为快速迭代⽽设计。

### Page 3

Codex 与 ChatGPT 的合并：从本地 Agent 到亿级⽤⼾
Tibo 将 Codex 并⼊ ChatGPT 的技术合并描述为⼀次重⼤⼯程挑战。
“ 这两套系统完全不同。 ChatGPT 是全托管云端架构； Codex 完全跑在本地。合并的核⼼问题
是：如何把这个本地编程 Agent 的能⼒，以⾜够⾼效的⽅式搬到云端，以便纳⼊每⽉ 20 美元的
Plus 套餐，服务数亿⽤⼾。 ”
⽬前， ChatGPT Work 本质上是在云端完整运⾏ Codex 的 harness ，配合⼀台⾼性能云端虚拟
机。 “ 有些⽤⼾已经发现，如果你⾜够有创意，可以让 ChatGPT 在⾥⾯训练另⼀个模型，安装
Blender 做 3D 建模 —— 它的权限⾮常开放。 ”
他透露，整个合并过程中， Codex 本⾝也充当了 “ 记者 ” ⻆⾊ —— 它接⼊了所有 Slack 频道和⽂
档，⾃动记录了整个项⽬的讨论与决策过程，形成了完整的叙事档案，内部称之为 “toggle
arc” 。
在未来⽅向上，他表⽰⽬前的 “Work 模式切换 ” 只是临时状态： “ 我们的⽬标是完全统⼀ —— 任何

在 Codex 能做的事，在 ChatGPT ⾥也能做，反之亦然。 ”
Tibo ⾃⼰怎么⽤ Codex
Tibo 描述了他的⽇常⼯作⽅式：⼤量使⽤⼿机端 ChatGPT Work ，在会议间隙⽤语⾳输⼊任务，
让 Agent ⽣成报告、整理信息、探索代码。
“ 我现在⼏乎任何问题都能在 30 分钟内得到答案。 ” 他说， “ ⽆论是某个功能的⽤⼾反馈、⽣产⽇志
的使⽤量、哪些功能应该下线 ——Codex 都能给我⼀个初步答案。 ”
他还提到⼀个习惯：把⽩天没时间深⼊的问题，晚上交给 Codex 跑，第⼆天早上看结果。 “ 这让
每个早晨都很令⼈期待。 ”
对于⼯程师担⼼⾃⼰的技能被 AI 取代， Tibo 的回应是： “ 代码是解决问题的⼯具。如果你真正在
乎的是结果和系统运转，那 AI 让你能解决更多问题，这应该让你成为更好的⼯程师。 ”
访谈全⽂如下：
与  Tibo Sottiaux 共谈  Codex 的构建历程

### Page 4

The Pragmatic Engineer Podcast
2026 年  9 ⽉  9 ⽇
Tibo Sottiaux 是  Codex 的创建⼯程师之⼀。如今，他负责  OpenAI 的核⼼产品与平台组织，该
组织也涵盖  Codex 。由于经常慷慨且频繁地发布  Codex 使⽤额度重置公告，他也是  Codex 最
为公众熟知的代表⼈物之⼀。
在本期《 The Pragmatic Engineer Podcast 》中， Tibo 与我讨论了  Codex 是如何构建、⼜如何
持续迭代的。我们探讨了  Codex CLI 为什么使⽤  Rust 编写并开源， Harness （代理运⾏框架）

“ 税 ” 。不过，我认为很多维护⼯作都会被⾃动化。

从童年、数学到创业
主持⼈  03:10
Tibo ，欢迎来到播客。很⾼兴邀请到你。

### Page 5

Tibo

Tibo 03:30

联⽹成了我学习了解世界的途径。
之后的经历基本都由此⽽来。我想，我得感谢⽗⺟把我带到那个偏僻的地⽅，让我别⽆选择，只

⾼中毕业后，你进⼊⼤学，开始系统学习这⽅⾯的知识，对吗？

DeepMind ⼯作，如今则来到了  OpenAI ，也就是加州。
我很喜欢加州的天⽓。这个话题我们之后可以再聊。

### Page 6

我们分析临床试验的供应链，尝试优化和判断：是否应该多⽣产⼀些药物？应该把药物运往哪
⾥、从哪⾥发货？如何避免浪费？通过这些⽅式提⾼临床试验的效率。

我们使⽤的是传统的⾮机器学习技术，主要是优化求解、蒙特卡洛模拟等⽅法。
本质上，这是⼀个随机多阶段优化问题。我们也将这套⽅法应⽤在钢铁⾏业和欧洲电⽹中。
只要⼀个问题具备优化问题的结构，我们就会对此感兴趣。直到今天，那家公司仍然存在，我认
为他们依旧在做⼀些⾮常有意思的⼯作。不过，随着现代  AI 的发展，这个领域当然也发⽣了很
⼤变化。
主持⼈  06:21

我的感觉是，你会不断深⼊⼀个问题领域：⾯对⼀个问题，运⽤⾃⼰学过或没学过的数学知识，

Tibo 06:41
对。这正是我痴迷于应⽤数学的原因。
理论数学、理论科学和物理学中，有些⼯作是为了发现未知的事物，或者探索其中的美。它们关

⽽现实世界中存在⼤量有趣的问题。我⾮常感兴趣的⼀点是：怎样让世界变得更好？怎样运⽤复
杂的数学⽅法，优化我们⾝边的世界？
这就是那家创业公司背后的许多核⼼理念。

### Page 7

那段经历⾮常有趣，因为我们是⼴告组织内部的⼀个⼩团队。这个项⽬的⽬标，是在流量向移动

我⼤概做了两年，后来项⽬被取消了。尽管那是我在解决⾼难度技术挑战⽅⾯最开⼼的⼀段经
历，但我也从中学到很多：例如，没有产品市场匹配度、没有正确的⽤⼾、没有有效的反馈闭环
时会发⽣什么；以及，当产品经理说项⽬进展顺利、但实际上进展很糟糕时，不应盲⽬信任。
有⼀天，⼀位副总裁从加州⻜来，告诉我们： “ 我们要取消这个项⽬。很遗憾，你们只有⼏百名
⽤⼾，这显然不符合  Google 的规模标准。 ”
令⼈难以置信的是，当时很多⼈感到惊讶。
这件事给我留下了⼀个⼀直带在⾝上的教训：始终保持质疑，始终深⼊思考你正在产⽣什么影
响，以及你所参与项⽬在整体中的重要性。
Tibo 09:20

### Page 8

后来有⼀个团队决定真正全⼒推进这个⽅向。对于我们这些为研究构建⼯具的⼈来说，很⾃然会

最终，你会得到⼀个聊天系统。

⽤，但和它们交互、不断尝试依然很有趣。
这个应⽤像野⽕⼀样传播开来，⼤家都在  DeepMind 内部分享各种对话记录。
Tibo 12:05
它感觉已经不只是⼀个研究项⽬，或者说不只是给研究⼈员使⽤的项⽬。
因此，随着时间推移，⼤家开始希望将它作为外部产品发布。但  DeepMind 并没有为此做好准
备。
在  Google 内部，产品发布有⼀套既定流程，有完整的⽣产体系和经过多年优化的标准技术栈。
它们确实很擅⻓把产品做好，但在这样的环境中进⾏真正的创新也很困难。
为什么加⼊  OpenAI
主持⼈  12:41
我想回到当时你的处境。假设是在  2023 年或  2024 年：你⾝处  Google ，那⾥持续发表出⾊论

⽂、开展优秀研究；你做的事情也⾮常有趣，正在不断突破此前的边界。
⽽且你已经在公司内部完成过⼀次转换。对于⼀个在当前岗位上感到舒适、满意的⼈来说 —— 我
想你当时应该也是如此 —— 是什么让你仍然会去探索其他可能性，甚⾄考虑  OpenAI ？
Tibo 13:02
我当时确实很舒适。 Google 是个好地⽅。
但我希望能遇到优秀的⼈，也希望加⼊⼀个我真正相信的使命。我希望与那些真正忠于这⼀使
命、深切关⼼如何以直接⽽深刻的⽅式为世界带来积极影响的⼈⼀起⼯作。
我不希望加⼊⼀个这样的组织：⼤家只是做⾃⼰的⼯作，然后把 “ 如何让它变得有⽤ ” 的问题交给
其他⼈。
我想加⼊⼀个能综合考虑所有变量的团队，⼀个研究与产品真正共同设计的团队。
当时  OpenAI 发展得⾮常好。 ChatGPT 正在起⻜。我认识了⼀些  OpenAI 的⼈，然后发现：
ChatGPT 团队居然只有⼤约  20 ⼈。
我当时想： “ 等等，什么？你们只有⼤约  20 ⼈在做  ChatGPT ？这个⼈数少得惊⼈。这⼀定意味

着极强的⾃主性。你们是怎么做到的？如何让只有  20 名⼯程师的团队维护如此规模的产品？ ”
随着不断深⼊了解，我发现那是⼀个⾮常出⾊的团队，有伟⼤的使命，也有极具天赋和驱动⼒的
⼈。
这些因素吸引了我。

### Page 9

Tibo 14:23
加⼊  OpenAI 后，我进⼊了推理模型发布前的相关⼯作。
这很符合  OpenAI 的⻛格：我刚加⼊不久，就有⼈说： “ 我们正在做⼀件事，准备发布推理模
型。这可能是⼀种新范式，开始冲刺吧。 ”
⼤约⼀个⽉后，公司就发布了  o1-preview 。
这让我感到⾮常振奋。我想加⼊的正是这样⼀个地⽅：⾏动迅速、在意影响、与世界保持同步、
真正倾听外界。
⽽这也是我后来在构建  Codex 、构建产品时⼀直坚持的理念：拥有社区，倾听社区，专注于建

Codex 的起点
主持⼈  15:10
之后，你很快开始参与  Codex 。你是在  2024 年加⼊  OpenAI 的。
能带我们回到当时吗？你加⼊时，⼤家对于  AI 、⼤语⾔模型和编程的看法是什么？

Tibo 15:34

对我⽽⾔，我最初加⼊时是为研究构建基础设施。我过去做过很多⼤规模数据存储、数据分析和

我们训练内部模型，让它们⾮常熟悉  OpenAI 的  Python 代码库，也让它们对架构、代码⻛格等
具备良好的判断⼒。
当时仅⽀持  Python 。想法是借助它快速构建基础设施，同时帮助研究⼈员更快地编写代码，从

⽽让我们整体推进得更快。

### Page 10

当不断推进并将事情简化到核⼼时，我们发现可以⾮常快速地取得进展、快速学习。

为什么  Codex 选择  Rust
主持⼈  18:25
你刚才提到，最初你们训练模型理解  Python 代码，帮助构建更好的基础设施。
但后来你们做了⼀个很有意思的决定： Codex 使⽤  Rust 构建。当时模型对  Rust 的⽀持并不像
对  Python 或  TypeScript 那样好。为什么做出这个决定？

是因为预期模型会赶上来？还是因为性能更重要？这个选择似乎与⼤多数其他框架不同。
Tibo 18:48
是的，⼤多数其他智能体框架并不是⽤  Rust 构建的，⽽是使⽤模型更擅⻓的  TypeScript 、
Python 或其他语⾔。
我们从第⼀性原理出发，很早就将产品界⾯与智能体本⾝视为不同的东西。
因此，我们认为必须以⼀种稳健、安全、⾯向效率和规模⽽设计的⽅式来构建智能体核⼼。
过去这些年，我参与过不少项⽬：⼀开始只是 “ ⼀个有趣的东西 ” ，后来却需要扩展到最⼤型数据
中⼼的规模。在这种情况下，早期技术决策最终会变得⾮常重要，前提是你不能因此牺牲过多的
开发速度。
这是⼀种取舍。
我们有很多⾮常⾼产、出⾊的  Rust 开发者。我们的内部模型虽然不算特别擅⻓  Rust ，但也并不
差。
此外， Rust 在编译期能提供⼤量验证：静态检查等。这些特性对智能体也很有帮助。
所以很快就变得清晰：如果投⼊⼀些精⼒， Rust 作为语⾔其实⾮常适合智能体。
我们的主要关注点是正确性和效率。

主持⼈  20:07

### Page 11

你的意思是，对于你们⽽⾔，值得提前思考产品最终要达到什么状态。
例如，在语⾔选择上，尽管有了智能体之后重写很多东西⽐过去容易，但如果⼀开始就建⽴正确

Tibo 20:28
对。我认为即使我们⽤  TypeScript ，甚⾄  Python 来写，最后也能成功。之后某个阶段，我们可

应有的程度更深。之后，这会阻碍进⼀步创新。
因此，从某种意义上说， Rust 的边界⾮常有⽤。
为什么  Codex 开源
主持⼈  21:08
你们做出的另⼀个有趣决定，在主要  AI 实验室中很独特：将这些组件开源。
CLI 、 SDK 和  App Server 都是开源的。你们是什么时候、⼜为什么做出这个决定？

架，⽽  Codex 是开放的。
为什么要开源？
Tibo 21:36
我们觉得，将代码开源是⼀件⾮常酷的事。
因为从根本上说，我们构建的是⼀个编程智能体。于是我们开始思考：既然有了它，当然会让它
指向⾃⾝；也许还可以建⽴⼀个贡献者社区，让⼤家⽤它来改进它，然后我们从中学习。
此外，当时我们很清楚：如果我们成功，开源本⾝以及代码本⾝的⻆⾊都会改变。
因此，成为这个社区的⼀部分，⽽不是与它脱节，是很重要的。
我认为，如果你不亲⾝经历问题，就很难真正解决问题。
还有⼀点是，当时虽然我们仍处于很早的阶段，但我们觉得⾃⼰对如何做好这件事有⼀些想法。
我们正与训练和研究共同设计这些能⼒，关键在于以最灵活、最好的⽅式表达模型能⼒。
但我们并没有所有答案。
因此，我们愿意公开地展⽰：⼀个好的框架是什么样的，我们如何思考它。我们发布了⼀些⾮常
技术化的深度解析和博客⽂章，也进⾏了很多讨论。
我们认为，外⾯的世界发展很快，有许多⾮常聪明的⼈。我们也会从其他开源项⽬中获得启发。

### Page 12

所以，不如建⽴⼀个公平的环境，在这个阶段⿎励⼤量尝试与探索。
主持⼈  23:17
如今已经过去⼀年到⼀年半了 —— 在  AI 的时间尺度⾥，这已经⾮常久。
回顾这段经历，开源给⼯程和⼯程团队带来了哪些好处？同时，开源⼜有哪些困难？我希望听到
两⽅⾯坦诚的看法。
Tibo 23:59
确实有缺点，开源是有成本的。
好处是：能够公开构建真的很棒。我们的仓库也很⼩，这很有价值。
每当我们招聘新⼈，加⼊  Codex 团队时，他们之前通常已经看过仓库、研究过  Pull Request 。
⼊职引导基本已经完成了。
真正的⼊职⽅式只是：⽤  Codex 浏览仓库、询问⼀些问题。但这不再是什么秘密，因此新⼈可
以⽴刻开始⾼效⼯作。
我们也收到了很多优质贡献。当然，也会收到海啸般的随机内容。
主持⼈
这显然也是你和所有其他开源项⽬都会遇到的问题。开源正在发⽣变化。
Tibo
没错。
对我和团队很多⼈⽽⾔，直接参与社区、直接作出贡献，会带来很⼤能量。这不只是⼝头上说
“ 我们重视社区 ” ，⽽是真正付出努⼒去做⼀些事情。我们并⾮必须这么做。

缺点则在于，开源部分与我们其他代码是分离的。因此，有时我们必须⼈为划分边界，并在多个
仓库之间协作，尤其是在开发⼀些特别令⼈兴奋的功能时。
另外，公开构建有时意味着：我们还没来得及发布，别⼈就已经复制了相关实现。
这会让⼈有点难受，但也是游戏规则的⼀部分。既然你选择公开构建，就相当于接受了这样的约
定：别⼈可以复制它。
我们的许可证也⾮常宽松。不过，当你正在做某个功能，却发现其他⼈已经将其复制并先⾏发
布，确实还是会有点刺痛。
第三点，和其他开源项⽬⼀样，我们会被⼤量随机贡献淹没，必须承担处理这些内容的额外成
本。
不过，这也会促使我们去解决这个问题，我认为这是⼀件好事。
为什么  Codex ⽀持其他模型

### Page 13

Tibo 26:16

这感觉并不对。

任何⼈都可以轻松地  Fork 它，然后只改⼗⾏代码就加⼊对其他模型提供商的⽀持。但如果这样

⽽这个  Fork 存在的唯⼀理由，仅仅是因为你不愿意改⼗⾏代码来⽀持另⼀个模型提供商。这很
没有必要。
为什么不从⼀开始就⽀持呢？
另外，我们从给⽤⼾提供选择中获益很多。

今天，你可能很喜欢  OpenAI 模型，并且使⽤它们⾮常⾼效；但明天可能发布⼀个新模型，你想
试试看。为什么要强迫你为了尝试⼀个新模型，就彻底更换整套⼯作环境？
我们还可以从这些反馈中获益。也许你喜欢另⼀个模型的某些特性；也许那个模型效果并不好，
但这依然能让我们获得信息。
善待⽤⼾和社区，是正确的做法。
我们⾃⼰也会尝试其他模型，也会在同⼀个框架中测试它们。这对所有⼈都有好处。
这种可选性对于与我们合作的企业也很重要。任何严肃的公司都希望保留选择权，因此我们会坚
定⽀持这⼀点。
主持⼈  28:16
我很欣赏这⼀点，因为它感觉⾮常坦诚。

Tibo 28:30

Codex 如何在本地与云端运⾏
主持⼈  31:50
作为⼯程师，我⼀直觉得，只要⼯具之间有竞争，作为⽤⼾就会受益。
我记得微软和  JetBrains 曾有  IDE 之战；云服务商也不断通过功能竞争；如今则有智能体框架
和模型之间的竞争。
作为⽤⼾，我们拥有更多选择，产品发展更快，我们的声⾳也能被更好地听到。
说到框架，能介绍⼀下  Codex 今天是如何运⾏的吗？
当我启动⼀个  Codex 任务时，它总是在我的机器上运⾏吗？它会选择云端吗？会使⽤沙箱吗？
我该如何控制或了解这些？作为⼯程师，我需要了解多少？
Tibo 32:28
默认情况下， Codex 在沙箱中运⾏。

如果某条命令需要在沙箱外获取额外权限， Codex 会向⽤⼾请求许可。但默认情况下，所有⼯
具执⾏都发⽣在沙箱内，并且完全在你的本地机器上运⾏。
这种模式已经持续⼀年多了。
不过，这也在不断演进。现在你可以选择在云端运⾏，它会在受管理的虚拟机中执⾏。这与
ChatGPT Work 所提供的是同⼀类虚拟机。
你可以查看这个环境，但它实际上运⾏在  Kata 容器中，是⼀个安全环境。所有操作都在该虚拟
机内部完成，不会在你的机器上执⾏。
你的本地设备只负责接收输⼊和流式返回输出。
这样当然会显著减轻你的  CPU 和机器负担，也能够扩展得更多。
这只是⼀个开始。未来，使⽤云端机器会更加⽆缝。你可能会同时使⽤笔记本上的部分执⾏和云
端机器上的部分执⾏。
随着模型越来越强⼤、越来越有能⼒，它们可以利⽤的计算资源和其他资源，远远超过本地机器
所能提供的范围。
因此，在某个阶段，仅将执⾏限制在本地机器上会成为⼀种约束。

### Page 15

主持⼈  34:15
本地运⾏有⼀个很⼤的优点，我想这也是我喜欢它的原因。
当然，它也会带来⿇烦。如果我同时运⾏⼏个智能体，它们会占⽤  CPU ；如果我想合上笔记

我曾去过⼀家  AI 公司的办公室，看到有⼈把笔记本半开着。他们问我： “ 你在运⾏智能体吗？ ”
我说： “ 对，我有⼀个任务正在跑。 ”

云端确实很棒，但它没有这些现成环境，或者说设置起来很⿇烦。
你们是否在考虑或试验如何处理这些环境配置？
这让我想起我们在  AI 出现之前讨论过的云开发环境。 2022 年、 2023 年它们很热⻔，但后来⼤
家开始更多讨论  AI 。
我觉得，除⼤型科技公司外，云端开发机并没有真正普及，因为前期设置成本很⾼，还要承担维

Tibo 35:21
是的。对于独⽴开发者或⼩团队来说，过去使⽤云开发环境的收益并不明显。
但有了现在的智能体能⼒之后，设置的成本⼏乎可以忽略不计。

如果智能体有能⼒完成环境设置，它就应该替你完成。
例如，你说⾃⼰有本地  SQLite 、⼀个本地服务器、 MCP 等环境。要在云开发机中配置完全相同
的环境，并让它们保持同步，到底有多难？
如果模型能替你完成这件事，那也许就不难。
我认为，我们将看到完全由云端编排的机器重新兴起，这将让你不再受笔记本电脑限制。
我们已经看到  ChatGPT Work 在这⽅⾯取得了巨⼤成功：它可以在⼿机上使⽤。
我每天早晨喝咖啡时，都会通过语⾳把⼀堆任务交给它。它会完成这些任务；它可以访问我的⽇
历、电⼦邮件和  Slack 。
能够边⾛边处理事务，不需要随⾝带着笔记本，感觉⾮常棒。
⽬前，我们交付的能⼒仍⽐较极端：执⾏依然发⽣在笔记本上。但如果你⽆需⼀直让笔记本保持
开启状态，那将⾮常美好。
Harness 与模型如何共同进化
主持⼈  36:41
你们过去是如何改进  Codex 的？

### Page 16

我记得⾃⼰第⼀次使⽤早期版本的  Codex 时，可以和它交谈，它也能完成⼀些任务。
但⽐如我说： “ 做这个改动。 ” 它完成了改动，可我有单元测试，它却没有运⾏。
之后⼏个⽉，不知道具体是哪天，它开始⾃动运⾏测试。
这些变化是怎样实现的？你们是在改进运⾏脚本、指令，还是某种启动脚本？还是在改进模型？
作为开发者，我该如何理解每个版本的进步 —— 框架与模型之间分别发⽣了什么？两者⼜是什么
关系？
Tibo 37:26
这是个好问题。
从某种意义上说，框架总是⽐模型领先⼀点。
主持⼈
这是什么意思？
Tibo
意思是：模型具备某些能⼒，但你会给它加上⼀些 “ 拐杖 ” ，让它能够以可靠、⾼效且符合⽤⼾预
期的⽅式完成任务。
这就是框架的作⽤。
它提供护栏、安全性，提升效率，并让智能体更容易被引导、控制和调整。
框架通常还负责所谓的 “ 开发者消息 ” ，也就是在每轮开始时注⼊上下⽂中的指令。这显然会影响
智能体在整个回合中的⾏为。
你看到的很多效果，都是框架与模型共同作⽤的结果。

代码审查的⻆⾊正在改变

代码审查的⻆⾊正在改变。

### Page 20

我早期参与  Codex 的⼀个项⽬，就是和研究团队⼀起开发⼀个代码审查模型。
我们的⽬标是让它达到这样的⽔平：它能够发现逻辑和推理中的错误，⽽⼈类可能需要数⼩时、
甚⾄多⼈投⼊才能发现同等程度的问题。
因为这可能需要向下深⼊三四层依赖关系，理解⽂档是否有误，理解某个第三⽅依赖的实现是否
与预期不同，进⽽发现你的不变量没有被维持，最终导致  Bug 。
除⾮你本⾝是那个库的专家，否则很难注意到这些问题。
我们开发了这些代码审查模型，也发布了它们。如今，它们发现错误、进⾏深度验证的能⼒已经
成为主线模型的⼀部分。
在基准测试中，它们在代码审查⽅⾯已经超越⼈类。
这不仅适⽤于正确性，也适⽤于安全性。
例如，模型能跨越⾮常复杂的上下⽂进⾏推理，最终指出： “ 这⾥存在⼀个严重的安全漏洞。 ”
如今，在  OpenAI 的所有  Pull Request 中，这已成为强制要求。如果系统标记出安全问题，我
们会阻⽌  Pull Request 合并。这⼀切都是⾃动完成的。
我认为，代码审查过去始终关乎正确性，确保功能正常运⾏；但它也是⼀种信息交流的仪式，使

⼈们保持同步，并⿎励讨论。
理想情况下，这些讨论应该在写代码之前进⾏，但有时它们只会围绕代码发⽣，因为⼀旦代码合
并，它就进⼊⽣产环境并开始实际运⾏，⽽之后你还必须维护它。
因此，其中还包含社会协作的因素。
我认为，正确性、⽹络安全和安全审查都会被⾃动化。
真正会保留下来的，是围绕意图展开的讨论：你到底想做什么？你尝试做的事情是否正确？
我认为，这种讨论可以发⽣在  Pull Request 之外，不必围绕代码本⾝。
也许这会帮助我们更清晰地区分：哪些地⽅真正需要讨论，⽽哪些地⽅过去只是因为没有现在这
样的⼯具，才不得不通过代码审查来进⾏讨论。
这会发⽣变化。
过去，代码审查是⼀个强制机制：因为在代码合并、进⼊⽣产环境前，你必须进⾏这些讨论。
但我认为，现在有其他⽅式来完成这些讨论、共同设计，并确保意图正确。代码本⾝的重要性将
不再那么⾼。
主持⼈  50:26

维护与重构成本下降

Tibo 52:30
维护本质上是⼀种⻓期⽀付的税，⽤来保证系统持续运⾏。
过去需要维护，未来仍然需要维护。

⽐如，你有⼀个第三⽅依赖，需要升级版本。过去你可能会想： “ 我得记得处理这件事。 ” 但现

### Page 22

过去，⼈们可能会把这件事搁置，因为它不够有趣；但它对业务和项⽬其实很重要，尤其涉及安

你希望保持最新状态，希望及时应⽤所有补丁。

有时甚⾄需要多年。
我认为，这件事现在也⼤幅加速了。

有时我仍会打开编辑器，亲⾃写⼀些代码。那种感觉很好。
我对⼀些夜晚有很美好的回忆：坐在  Vim 前不断编码，喝着可⼝可乐  Zero ，不必思考其他任何
事情，只专注于眼前的问题。
但我认为，真正重要的是进⼊⼼流、解决问题。
我发现，这⾥的同事，以及我交流过的所有⼈，都在⾮常迅速地适应。
如果你的⼼态是：代码只是解决问题的⼯具，⽽现在你能解决的问题多得多，那么变化就是积极
的。
过去，如果你想做个基准测试，却不确定结果如何，你可以直接让它在后台执⾏。可能只需要
30 秒，就能获得可靠数字，从⽽做出更好的权衡。
只要你真正关⼼结果、关⼼系统是否正常⼯作，这应该会让你成为更好的⼯程师。

### Page 24

在  OpenAI ，这使我们能更⾼效地运⾏推理，更有效地利⽤计算资源，并将这些能⼒交付给世
界。
因此，⼤家都专注于这⼀点：以过去不可能达到的速度解决重要问题。
到⽬前为⽌，我还没有遇到有⼈说： “ 这不好，这不有趣。 ”
主持⼈  59:10
我理解的是，如果你有⾜够宏⼤的问题，有远超过今天、明天或下周所能解决的问题，这就不是
问题。
因为当你在某个⽅⾯变得更⾼效后，你只会继续向前。
这就像很多创业公司：它们的抱负总是远⼤于⾃⼰当下能完成的事情。
Tibo 59:32
没错。我们绝对不缺问题要解决，⽽且我认为短期内也不会缺。
我们在数学突破、科学突破、让世界变得更好、真正为⼈类构建产品，以及以深刻⼈性的⽅式解
决⼈们最重要的问题⽅⾯，还有很⻓的路要⾛。
这正是我们在这⾥的原因。
Tibo 01:00:00
再回到编程和那些深夜。
我刚才讲的，可能有些浪漫化。事实上，我也经历过很多深夜，试图重构某件事，三个⼩时后才
发现这条路根本⾛不通，必须从头开始。
那⾮常令⼈沮丧。
当然有快乐的时刻，但也有那种代码⽆法编译的时刻，你不断想： “ 为什么它还是编译不过？ ”

主持⼈  01:00:30
我相信你也经历过这种情况：时间已经很晚了，你该睡觉了，但因为有⼀项任务只做了⼀半，根
本睡不着。
我记得⾃⼰甚⾄会梦到代码。
⽽如今，我在为⾃⼰的业务开发软件时，通常不会再有那种 “ 只完成⼀半 ” 的状态。因为我可以让
智能体完成任务，然后离开；要么任务已经完成，要么它在运⾏，要么我已经得到失败的证据。
⼀切都加速了。
Tibo 01:01:13
我现在仍然会有类似的情况。

### Page 25

很多⼈都会这么做，我⾃⼰也是如此：有时我会思考⼀些更⼤的问题，可能来⾃⽩天的对话，或
者我还没来得及深⼊研究。
于是，我会让  Codex 在夜间研究这些问题。
第⼆天醒来查看结果时，我会⾮常兴奋。因此，每个早晨都令⼈期待。
主持⼈  01:01:34
我觉得这就形成了⼀种⻓时间运⾏任务的模式。
当然，你可以使⽤  /goal 命令来持续运⾏任务。这也是⼏个⽉前才加⼊  Codex 的能⼒。
回到 “ 框架是拐杖 ” 这个概念，/goal 是否就是⼀种拐杖？它是否是为了让模型在很⻓⼀段时间⾥
专注于单⼀⽬标⽽设计的？
Tibo 01:01:56
是的。/goal 是必要的，它让模型能够⻓期围绕⼀个⽬标保持正轨。
它甚⾄可以让模型在⾯对真正困难的问题时，持续运⾏数天或数周。
但随着新⼀代模型出现，我们看到的是：你不再需要  /goal，也不再需要围绕它的额外框架。
你可以直接告诉模型： “ 去⼯作⼀周。 ” 它确实能做到。
ChatGPT 与  Codex 的合并
主持⼈  01:02:21

说到难题，以及你们并不缺少难题，有⼀个从外部看来很有意思的发布：你们所说的 “ 合并 ” 。
Codex 出现在  ChatGPT 中。
从 ⼯ 程 师 的 视 ⻆ 看 ， 我 会 说 这 件 事 中 等 程 度 地 有 趣 。 我 们 已 经 在 ⽤  Codex ； 现 在 可 以 在
ChatGPT 应⽤⾥打开它，很好。
但我通常不会在应⽤⾥使⽤  ChatGPT ，⽽是直接进⼊  Codex 。
不过，我和  OpenAI 的很多⼈、包括你团队的⼈交流过，他们说这项合并前进⾏了很多准备，⾯
临很多持久且艰巨的挑战。
能否介绍⼀下：这究竟是⼀个多⼤的项⽬？你们需要做什么？为什么难？ Codex 及其他⼯具⼜
如何帮助你们以过去难以实现的⽅式完成它？
⾃从合并推出后，你不断分享  Codex ⽤⼾数量增⻓的数据，增⻓速度⽐过去快得多。我猜这⾥
解决了巨⼤的扩展问题。
Tibo 01:03:25
这次合并有很多挑战。
⾸先，两者的技术栈完全不同。

### Page 26

ChatGPT 是完全托管的、基于云端的产品。所有东西都运⾏在我们的系统上，我们以传统的⼤
规模、⾼效率⽅式存储和构建它。
⽽  Codex 是完全本地化的。
因此，合并的核⼼在于：如何让这个本地编程智能体获得同样的价值与能⼒，并围绕它构建⼀个
产品，使其能够惠及更⼴泛的⼈群。
这也是我们加⼊  OpenAI 的原因：希望让⾮常⼴泛的全球⼈群从中受益。
因此，这是⼀个令⼈兴奋的探索过程：怎样构建⼀个云端版本，使它在本质上具备⾮常相似的能
⼒；同时，⼜能够以⾼效的⽅式服务数千万、数亿⽤⼾，从⽽可以将它包含在  Plus 订阅⽅案
中。
ChatGPT Work 本质上是在云端运⾏完整的  Codex 框架，并配备⼀台云端计算机。
它实际上是⼀台⾮常强⼤的机器。⼈们已经发现并展⽰了它能做什么。
如果你的提⽰词⾜够有创造⼒，可以让  ChatGPT 在其中训练另⼀个模型；也可以让它安装
Blender ，进⾏  3D 建模。
它的权限⾮常宽松，能访问互联⽹，是⼀台功能强⼤的机器。 Codex 在这台机器上运⾏。

这就是我们通过  ChatGPT Work 交付的能⼒。
这其中涉及很多系统层⾯的挑战。
团队完成得很快。显然， Codex 帮助我们更⾼效地分析并构建⼤量基础设施，也帮助我们处理
Codex 与  ChatGPT 之间出现的很多细微差异。
例如，合并插件架构、合并库，并真正朝着统⼀系统迈进。
我们的⽬标是：⽤⼾不应该觉得有些事只能在  Codex 中做、不能在  ChatGPT 中做，或者反过
来。
我们希望构建⼀个统⼀产品，让你以⾃⼰希望的⽅式，访问同样的智能。
Tibo 01:06:05
整个过程中还有⼀件很有趣的事： Codex 也充当了记者。
它记录了整个过程中的步骤、争论和讨论。团队之间针对如何实施、如何命名、何时引⼊、以什
么形式引⼊、什么与什么合并，进⾏了⾮常热烈的讨论。
我们考虑过许多不同⽅案。
因此，这个项⽬有很有趣的新闻记录元素： Codex 随着时间推移记录了整个过程。
这也逐渐被称为  OpenAI 的 “ 切换开关故事线 ” 。
我们引⼊了  Work 开关。围绕它是否是正确做法，也有过⼤量讨论。但后来，我们逐渐真正喜欢

上了它。

### Page 27

随着时间推移，我们会进⼀步合并这些能⼒。
我们正朝着完全统⼀的⽅向前进。⽬前的状态只是暂时的：当你处于  Work 模式时，能⼒会更
强；但⻓期来看，我们会将这些能⼒带给所有  ChatGPT ⽤⼾。
Tibo 如何使⽤  Codex
主持⼈  01:07:13
你个⼈如何使⽤  Codex ？
你的⼯作配置是什么样的？你如何使⽤智能体、处理哪些任务、管理哪些事情？
相关地，我问过  Peter Steinberger 我应该问你什么。他说： “ 你⼀定要问他，他参与那么多项
⽬，⽇历像俄罗斯⽅块⼀样排满，但通常还显得很愉快。他是怎么做到的？ ”
Tibo 01:07:33
我的⽇历还好。
如今我能够完成更多事情，是因为有了  Codex 这样的技术。
我已经把很多⼯作转移到⼿机上，通过  ChatGPT Work 完成。
每当我想记录⼀件事，我就直接把它发出去。我经常使⽤语⾳输⼊。
每当我有问题，以前我可能会写下来，之后再研究，或者委托给别⼈；现在，我会直接在
ChatGPT Work 中把任务发出去，然后得到⼀份报告。

它拥有许多⾃定义技能和⾃定义指令，现在已经⾮常适合为我⽣成我能⾼效吸收的报告、幻灯⽚
和代码探索结果。
因此，每当我在会议之间有空档，你可能会看到我对着⼿机说话。
正如我前⾯提到的，我们在公开频道中完成许多⼯作。 Slack 中有很多信息， Notion 和  Google
Docs 中也有很多信息。
所以，⼏乎不存在我觉得⽆法提出的问题。 Codex ⾄少可以对任何问题进⾏第⼀轮思考。
⽆论是分析某个功能的公众情绪，查看⽣产⽇志以了解某项功能的使⽤量，列出应该淘汰的、没
有获得⽤⼾认可的内容，还是了解某个团队正在做什么 —— 任何问题，我都可以在⼤约  30 分钟
内获得答案。
所以，我⽤它来做所有事情。它在各种意义上都是我的个⼈智能体。
周末时，我也常做⼀些代码探索或构建原型，尝试想象产品的未来。我会和团队中的其他⼈⼀起
做这些事，不⼀定总是同⼀个团队。
有时，⼀天之内，我就可以构建出⼀些原本只存在于脑海中的东西。
⽐如有⼀天早上醒来，我会想： “ 我们应该探索⼀下构建这个东西意味着什么。 ”

### Page 28

然后，我就能把这些想法表达出来，在⼀天内做出⼀个能展⽰给其他⼈的东西。⼤家可以据此思
考、批评，并希望从中获得启发。
这并不意味着我们⼀定要发布它。
更像是：我把这个想法从脑海中释放出来，然后继续做其他事情。
这是⼀个⾮常神奇的时代，也⾮常赋能。
给软件⼯程师的建议
主持⼈  01:10:19
最后，如果⼀位软件⼯程师、 AI ⼯程师，或者任何构建软件的⼈，希望培养所需的技能和经
验，以便有机会加⼊  Codex 团队、 OpenAI 或⼀家  AI 创业公司，成为⼀名善于使⽤这些⼯具的
优秀构建者，你有什么建议？
⼈们经常问：我应该从理论开始吗？基础知识有多重要？还是只要⾮常擅⻓使⽤⼯具就可以？
Tibo 01:10:41
我认为有两件事很重要。
第⼀，是对事物如何运作抱有深度好奇⼼；第⼆，是训练⾃⼰快速理解事物的能⼒。
事情会持续变化，但在  OpenAI 表现极其出⾊的⼈，通常是那些能够迅速理解⼀个系统、进⼊新
的代码库并理清其脉络的⼈。
当然，如今智能体能够帮助完成这些⼯作。
你需要吸收的信息⾮常多，因此，能够理解并推理这些信息⾮常重要。

其中很⼤⼀部分，其实是提出好问题：事物到底是如何运作的？
不断追问 “ 五个为什么 ” ，持续向下挖掘。这样你会学得⾮常快。
第⼆点，是与社区保持同步，或者说，与那些你试图为其解决问题的⼈保持同步。
并不是每件事都在解决⼀个直接的问题。有时，你解决的是⼀个对另⼀群⼈有⽤的问题，⽽他们
最终也在为⼈类问题的解决作出贡献。
但你必须清楚地理解⽤⼾的品味、需求和要求；必须能够清晰思考，并持续训练这种思维清晰
度。
这对我来说⾮常重要。
如果你⽆法解释⾃⼰想实现什么，⽆法解释⾃⼰的意图，⽆法与某个社区建⽴联系，也没有相应
的品味，那么你就很难做出伟⼤的⼯作。
主持⼈  01:12:05
太棒了， Tibo 。⾮常感谢这次对话，真的很精彩。

### Page 29

Tibo
感谢邀请。
节⽬总结
主持⼈  01:12:16
我⼀直想和  Tibo 深⼊聊⼀次，很⾼兴终于实现了。
我很欣赏  Tibo 不仅谈论开源的好处，也坦率谈到了它的缺点。
尤其是，当你正在公开开发某项功能时，竞争对⼿可能复制这个功能，并在你正式发布前抢先上
线。这确实会令⼈不舒服。
另外，开源还会带来⼤量低质量贡献，⽽你仍然必须以某种⽅式处理它们。
另⼀个有趣的观点是， Tibo 说框架总是⽐模型领先⼀步。
从内部来看， Codex 团队认为⾃⼰的⼯作，是通过框架、⼯具和设置指令为模型构建 “ 拐杖 ” 。随
后，下⼀版本的模型会被训练得不再需要那么多拐杖。
坦率说，作为开发者，这听起来有⼀点让⼈泄⽓：我构建的东西，下⼀版模型可能天然就会知
道，于是就能被移除。
不过，我怀疑事情并不只是构建这些拐杖，也包括构建模型会使⽤的⼯具。下⼀代模型应该不会
重新发明  MCP 协议、技能或插件。⾄少我希望不会。
我也很喜欢了解从内部看，合并  ChatGPT 和  Codex 是怎样的过程。

这意味着要将原本完全本地化的编程智能体  Codex ，整合进⼀个托管式云端技术栈；同时还要
做到⾜够⾼效，使其能够纳⼊  OpenAI 每⽉  20 美元的订阅⽅案。考虑到  20 美元能购买的计算
资源并不算多，这并不容易。
还有⼀件很有趣的事： Codex 本⾝充当了整个项⽬的记者。由于它出现在所有  Slack 对话和⽂
档中，因此能够记录所有重要争论和决策。
我不得不承认，这⼀部分有⼀点 “ ⽼⼤哥 ” 的感觉： AI ⼀直在看。
但这很可能会成为未来创业公司的新常态。我还没有决定⾃⼰对此究竟是什么感受。
最后，我很认可  Tibo 给⼯程师的建议：保持好奇⼼，快速理解系统，并与⾃⼰正在服务的⼈群
保持同步。
从  Tibo 的分享中听到基础能⼒依然如此重要，也令⼈感到安⼼。
请查看下⽅节⽬说明，其中包含关于  Codex 、 Claude Code 、 Cursor 构建⽅式及其他相关主题
的深度解析。
如果你喜欢本期内容，请在你使⽤的播客播放器中留下评分。这对我和节⽬都意义重⼤。

谢谢，我们下期再⻅。

### Page 30

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

implied credit quality spans from the 97th percentile (strongest) to the 2nd (weakest), 
with the averag
 e sitting near the 62nd 
 — 
barely better than a median credit.  
In effect, 
the rank bought by rising market 
 capitalizations  
and compressed volatility is being 
spent on debt growth, 
 both 
recognized  
and hidden. The two legs are not symmetric 
 – 
debt is contractual and accumulates on schedule, while equity 
 relies on  
the 
future
.  
• 
 A severe market repricing 
 – 
equity down 10%, volatility up 10pp, debt up 50% 
 – 
would 
 cost the panel two standard deviations of distance
 -
to
-
default, 
 translating into 
~ 
20bps  
of model sprea
 d
. 
Debt sensitivity is actually lower: recognizing leases 
 – 
equivalent to 150% of debt 
 – 
moves spreads by only ~10bps, with wide dispersion 
across names. Mid
 -
2022 is the natural stress test: the same balance sheets, carrying far 
less debt, still showed vulner
 ability 
 – 
volatility and time horizon matter more than rates 
or equity levels  
.  
• 
 As t
ech allocation in credit portfolios grow
 s
, spreads 
 do not fully compensate 
 for 
skewed risk.  
Default risk is 
 still remote
 , 
but spread risk 
 will 
increasingly surface.  
The 
liability  
leg is contractual and arrives on schedule
 , while 
the equity leg can reverse in a 
quarter
 . 
Tech’s curve steepness 
 already prices the long horizon as 
 the 
key risk
 , and  
that 
may 
 extend 
 to the front
-
end
. 
Tech has avoided the iceberg so far, but the navigation is 
getting trickier
 , and the metric to watch is 
 spread vs. 
 quality 
 ranking 
 within the 
corporate market
 , 
including 
 by financial  
commitments 
 and 
 equity volatility

From the broad market to the 
 (
A
)
Iceberg  
What is the right price for carrying the 
 tail risk of the AI build
 -
out? 
W
e put th
 at 
question to structural credit models  
that 
price default risk from the equity market and the balance sheet
 , 
rather than from credit markets themselves
 . 
By definition,  
equity keeps the upside if the 
 AI 
build
 -
out works, credit 
mainly 
 absorbs the loss if it does not. 
 The mega
 -
cap technology complex already dominates the equity indices and is now ballooning in credit to finance that build
 -
out
. 
A
nd investment
 -
grade spreads offer only modest compensation  
of 
~
80
bps
 , 
even a
 fter the tech
-
led widening of 
2026.

The starting point is Merton (1974), and behind the mathematics sits a simple idea
 : 
a 
company owns assets and 
owes debt. If the assets are worth more than the debt when the bill comes due, shareholders keep the difference; if 
they are not, creditors take what is left. Owning the shares is therefore a bet on the assets clearing the debt 
 – 
and 
that means the equity market is already saying something about default risk. Two things drive the answer: how 
much cushion sits be
 tween what a company is worth and what it owes, and how fast that cushion can move, which 
is what equity volatility measures. A thick cushion that barely moves is safe. A thin one that swings around is not.  
The model implicitly assumes 
 the consensus  
AI narrative  
embedded in equity
 , 
with 
equity volatility taken as a proxy 
for the market's uncertainty.  
This approach 
 may not fully appreciate the 
 income from 
 current AI
 -
investment
 . 
B
ut 
credit investors will barely benefit  
from it, 
and 
 instead 
 need to focus on do
 wnside against 
 slim spread.

Merton’s limitation is that it is too well
 -
behaved at the top end. Give it a company with 2% leverage and it reports a 
default risk of essentially zero 
 – 
hard to argue 
 and 
 work with. 
A 
KMV
 -
inspired 
 approach
 1 
– 
our own implementation 
of the published structural framework, not the proprietary Moody’s Analytics KMV  
model 
 – 
keeps that logic and 
fixes the last step: instead of reading default risk off a bell curve, it reads it off what has actually happened 
 in the 
past 
 to companies 
 with similar cushions
 . 
I
t 
also 
 assumes a 
 that 
 firm has to cover its short
 -
term debt plus 
 part of  
its 
long
 -
term debt
 , 
rather than everything  
all  
at once. The output arrives in a language credit investors already use 
 – 
an implied rating. That matters here, because when Merton has flattened to zero, the rating still moves. 
CreditGrades, a variant that allows for uncertainty about exactly where the d
 efault point sits, is run alongside as a 
sanity check.

We run 
both 
models  
on 
key technology 
 companies  
– 
Microsof
 t, Apple, Alphabet, Amazon, Meta
 , 
Nvidia, 
 and  
Oracle
 . 
SpaceX  
was also 
 added 
 in 
recent analysis
 , completing the eight
 -
name panel we refer to throughout
 . 
The analysis 
covers 
 five timestamps from mid
 -
2022 
 – 
the onset of the AI theme 
 – 
to mid
 -
2026, using the market 
 capitalization
 , 
equity volatility and risk
 -
free rate prevailing at each date. Each name is priced under two 
 debt  
barriers: 
 reported 
balance
 -
sheet 
financial debt only, 
 versus  
balance
 -
sheet 
debt plus 
 off
-
balance sheet  
commitments 
 – 
together
 , 
the 
(
A
)
Ice
berg.

Executive Summary 
— Policy incentives and spending hikes are driving the expansion of the global 
military drone market, with a projected CAGR of 8% through 2030. This looks 
Multi-billion-dollar investment pledges in the UK, US, Japan, India, and the 
EU are among those that reflect this rapid build-out.  
set to unlock new investor opportunities across the wider drone ecosystem. 
— The surge in global spending will likely benefit established manufacturers in 
markets including the US, UK, and Türkiye. Ukrainian companies are well-
positioned to capture post-war market share, as their footprint expands 
across Europe, the Gulf, and Asia-Pacific. Japan and the Philippines are 
among Asian markets interested in Ukrainian capabilities. 
— The rapid development of drone technology – particularly hardware – risks 
leaving governments with obsolete stockpiles. For investors, this makes the 
sector harder to assess than the headline growth numbers suggest. 
Companies able to upgrade quickly across many drone models, or those 
specialising in software that can be rapidly upgraded, may be better 
positioned than those tied to a single platform.

the technology, creating localised security risks that could drive further 
spending and prompt new restrictions on commercial drone use.

### Page 2

7 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong
Context
On 2 September, Ukraine announced it would soon finalise a bilateral drone
production and development deal with Germany. This followed the signing of drone
production and export agreements with Denmark, the Netherlands, and Estonia on
the sidelines of the NATO Summit in July. The deals are among the latest
developments marking Kyiv’s emergence as one of the global leaders in drone

technology. The agreements also come against the backdrop of a worldwide surge
in investment in the sector. This trend looks set to continue for years, as
governments absorb the lessons of the conflicts in Ukraine and the Middle East.
This report assesses the key geopolitical implications of the accelerating global race
for drone dominance.
Disrupting Defence: The Rise of Cost-Effective Drone Power
The proliferation of inexpensive drones is reshaping military doctrine, defence

spending, and procurement worldwide. While military drones date back decades,
the technology has reached a number of major milestones and ‘firsts’ in the last
several years, reflecting the accelerating pace of development, production and
deployment (see timeline). The wars in Ukraine and Iran in particular have
demonstrated the limitations of traditional costly military assets in the face of
numerous, increasingly capable and AI-enabled, and often disposable drones.

Figure 1: Selected key milestones in military drone production and use, 2020-2026
Source : Deutsche Bank Research
Drones are unlikely to fully displace other military assets, including the need for
‘boots on the ground’ to seize and hold territory, but the next several years will likely
see a global push toward adoption and integration with conventional military forces
on an unprecedented scale.

### Page 3

7 September 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 3
Ukraine: From Necessity to Supremacy
Ukrainian companies could capture a sizeable share of the expanding market
after the war. The need to counter Russia’s manpower advantages has driven an
innovation model linking frontline demand with fast domestic production. This has
established Ukraine as a global leader in unmanned operations and high-volume
production, particularly of attack drones. This includes utilising AI for navigation,

targeting, and autonomous “last mile” attacks.
Kyiv reportedly doubled its drone procurement y/y during H1 and plans to
eventually use robots for 100% of frontline logistical needs. According to President
Zelenskyy, Ukraine now produces 10 million drones per year (likely more than all of
NATO combined, although Ukrainian figures probably include a large proportion of
small tactical drones), and the country could scale production to 20 million units if

its international partners provide sufficient funding. Nevertheless, once active
combat ends and stockpiling needs are met, production could slow given how
quickly drone capabilities change (see below).
Ukraine’s manufacturing footprint is likely to extend further beyond its borders.
It is already expanding to facilities in countries including the UK, Denmark, Norway,
and Germany, fostering technological diffusion and the emergence of a Europe-
wide supply chain. Ukraine is also pursuing joint production with countries in the

Middle East, Europe and the Caucasus, and has struck 10-year cooperation
agreements with Saudi Arabia, the UAE and Qatar. More recently, the WSJ
reported on 21 July that a new joint venture will produce Ukrainian sea-based attack
drones in the US.
A Worldwide Surge in Spending
Policy incentives and spending hikes are set to fuel an expansion of drone and
counter-drone arsenals in the coming years. The global military drone market is
expected to grow at a CAGR of 8% through 2030, with growth in some regions –

such as the Asia Pacific and Europe, the latter with an expected CAGR of 13% –
projected to be even higher, according to Global Market Insights. The forecasts
signal robust, sustained expansion as global defence forces accelerate their
transition to unmanned platforms. This trajectory should create opportunities for
investors and companies in the wider ecosystem – from drone manufacturers to
component, technology and software suppliers, as well as contractors providing

support and maintenance.

### Page 4

7 September 2026
Thematic Research
Page 4 Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong
Figure 2: The global drone market is expected to grow by a CAGR of 8% by 2030
Source : Global Market Insights, Deutsche Bank
Recent key developments include record procurement and spending requests in
the US – already the world’s largest military drone market. Other notable initiatives
include a historic shift toward a Hybrid Navy in the UK, the creation of a “Drone
Wall” on the EU’s eastern flank, a mass purchase of commercial drones for military

use in South Korea, and a record US$2 billion investment push in India, which aims
to bolster domestic drone manufacturing and reduce reliance on foreign suppliers
(see table).
These investment pledges – set against the backdrop of rising global defence
spending, which reached US$2.6 trillion in 2025 – are set to boost demand among
existing manufacturers beyond Ukraine, including established players in the US
and the UK. This trend is reflected in Türkiye’s recent export boom. Türkiye is the

world’s largest military drone supplier, accounting for some 65% of armed-drone
export sales. The country saw its defence exports triple over the past five years, and
reportedly aims to double them in the next two.
The Asia-Pacific will likely continue to emerge as a major market.  Geopolitical
tensions in the Taiwan Strait and the South China Sea could see more investment
in surface and undersea drone capabilities . Ukrainian defence companies also

reportedly aim to expand manufacturing and technological cooperation into the
region, with Japan, the Philippines and other regional economies among potential
markets. Regional companies are also seeking Ukrainian expertise, as reflected by
a recent Japanese acquisition of two Ukrainian drone startups.

### Page 5

7 September 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG/Hong Kong Page 5
Figure 3: Selected recent developments, including planned or proposed spending increases
Source : Deutsche Bank Research, external sources (see links in table).
Tech Turnover: The Obsolescence Dilemma
The scramble for drones risks leaving governments with stockpiles of obsolete
equipment. The technological ecosystem around drones, and counter-drone
systems, is not yet mature. Battlefield pressure forces rapid innovation, sometimes

within weeks, risking large stockpiles becoming quickly obsolete. The rapid growth
of the industry, and the proliferation of new companies entering the sector, also
raises questions about the risks of an eventual industry consolidation.
For investors, this makes the sector harder to assess than the headline growth
numbers suggest. The better-positioned companies may be those that can
upgrade quickly across many drone models – through software, flexible or modular

manufacturing and battlefield feedback – rather than those tied to a single drone
platform. With hardware vulnerable to obsolescence, software could prove to be a
material differentiating element, and several manufacturers are prioritising
software platforms as key to upgrading their systems.
Rogue Drones: The Proliferation to Non-State Groups
Drone technology is a ‘great equaliser’ for non-state groups that could fuel a rise
in global security risks. Organised crime, terrorist, and insurgent groups have

expanded their drone capabilities in recent years. Groups like Hezbollah, Hamas,
guerrillas in Colombia, and Mexican drug cartels have already adapted cheap
commercial drones for attacks and demonstrated an ability to absorb the lessons
of the war in Ukraine.
The proliferation of drone technology means that defences must cover a wide range
of potential targets – from critical infrastructure to public events and government
buildings – while being updated to close gaps that small, adaptive groups can

exploit. Although there is little evidence to suggest that these groups have used AI-
